<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1033289096">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">For the former North American fur-trading district, see <a href="/wiki/New_Caledonia_(Canada)" title="New Caledonia (Canada)">New Caledonia (Canada)</a>. For the former colony of Scotland, see <a href="/wiki/Darien_scheme" title="Darien scheme">Darien scheme</a>.</div>

<p><b>New Caledonia</b> (<span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˌ/: secondary stress follows">ˌ</span><span title="&#39;k&#39; in &#39;kind&#39;">k</span><span title="/æ/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;bad&#39;">æ</span><span title="&#39;l&#39; in &#39;lie&#39;">l</span><span title="/ɪ/: &#39;i&#39; in &#39;kit&#39;">ɪ</span><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;d&#39; in &#39;dye&#39;">d</span><span title="/oʊ/: &#39;o&#39; in &#39;code&#39;">oʊ</span><span title="&#39;n&#39; in &#39;nigh&#39;">n</span><span title="/i/: &#39;y&#39; in &#39;happy&#39;">i</span><span title="/ə/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;about&#39;">ə</span></span>/</a></span></span> <a href="/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key" title="Help:Pronunciation respelling key"><i title="English pronunciation respelling"><span style="font-size:90%">KAL</span>-ih-<span style="font-size:90%">DOH</span>-nee-ə</i></a>; <a href="/wiki/French_language" title="French language">French</a>: <i lang="fr">Nouvelle-Calédonie</i> <small></small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="fr-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/French" title="Help:IPA/French">[nuvɛl kaledɔni]</a></span>&#32;<span data-nosnippet="" id="ooui-php-1" class="ext-phonos ext-phonos-PhonosButton noexcerpt ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel oo-ui-widget oo-ui-widget-enabled oo-ui-buttonElement oo-ui-buttonElement-frameless oo-ui-iconElement oo-ui-buttonWidget" data-ooui="{&quot;_&quot;:&quot;mw.Phonos.PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;\/\/upload.wikimedia.org\/wikipedia\/commons\/transcoded\/d\/dc\/LL-Q150_%28fra%29-GrandCelinien-Nouvelle-Cal%C3%A9donie.wav\/LL-Q150_%28fra%29-GrandCelinien-Nouvelle-Cal%C3%A9donie.wav.mp3&quot;,&quot;rel&quot;:[&quot;nofollow&quot;],&quot;framed&quot;:false,&quot;icon&quot;:&quot;volumeUp&quot;,&quot;data&quot;:{&quot;ipa&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;text&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;lang&quot;:&quot;en&quot;,&quot;wikibase&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;file&quot;:&quot;LL-Q150 (fra)-GrandCelinien-Nouvelle-Cal\u00e9donie.wav&quot;},&quot;classes&quot;:[&quot;ext-phonos&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;noexcerpt&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel&quot;]}"><a role="button" tabindex="0" href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/d/dc/LL-Q150_%28fra%29-GrandCelinien-Nouvelle-Cal%C3%A9donie.wav/LL-Q150_%28fra%29-GrandCelinien-Nouvelle-Cal%C3%A9donie.wav.mp3" rel="nofollow" aria-label="Play audio" class="oo-ui-buttonElement-button"><span class="oo-ui-iconElement-icon oo-ui-icon-volumeUp"></span><span class="oo-ui-labelElement-label"></span><span class="oo-ui-indicatorElement-indicator oo-ui-indicatorElement-noIndicator"></span></a></span><sup class="ext-phonos-attribution noexcerpt"><a href="/wiki/File:LL-Q150_(fra)-GrandCelinien-Nouvelle-Cal%C3%A9donie.wav" title="File:LL-Q150 (fra)-GrandCelinien-Nouvelle-Calédonie.wav">i</a></sup>)<sup id="cite_ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5">&#91;nb 1&#93;</a></sup> is a <a href="/wiki/Sui_generis_collectivity" class="mw-redirect" title="Sui generis collectivity"><i>sui generis</i> collectivity</a> of <a href="/wiki/Overseas_France" title="Overseas France">overseas France</a> in the southwest <a href="/wiki/Pacific_Ocean" title="Pacific Ocean">Pacific Ocean</a>, south of <a href="/wiki/Vanuatu" title="Vanuatu">Vanuatu</a>, about 1,210&#160;km (750&#160;mi) east of <a href="/wiki/Australia" title="Australia">Australia</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-enc-pres_6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-enc-pres-6">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> and 17,000&#160;km (11,000&#160;mi) from <a href="/wiki/Metropolitan_France" title="Metropolitan France">Metropolitan France</a>. The archipelago, part of the <a href="/wiki/Melanesia" title="Melanesia">Melanesia</a> subregion, includes the main island of <a href="/wiki/Grande_Terre_(New_Caledonia)" title="Grande Terre (New Caledonia)">Grande Terre</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Loyalty_Islands" class="mw-redirect" title="Loyalty Islands">Loyalty Islands</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Chesterfield_Islands" title="Chesterfield Islands">Chesterfield Islands</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Belep" title="Belep">Belep</a> archipelago, the <a href="/wiki/Isle_of_Pines_(New_Caledonia)" title="Isle of Pines (New Caledonia)">Isle of Pines</a>, and a few remote islets.<sup id="cite_ref-pres-om_7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pres-om-7">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> The Chesterfield Islands are in the <a href="/wiki/Coral_Sea" title="Coral Sea">Coral Sea</a>. French people, especially locals, call Grande Terre "<span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Le Caillou</i></span>" ("the pebble").<sup id="cite_ref-Stanley1989_8-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Stanley1989-8">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>New Caledonia has a land area of 18,576&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (7,172&#160;sq&#160;mi) divided into three <a href="/wiki/Province" title="Province">provinces</a>. The <a href="/wiki/North_Province,_New_Caledonia" title="North Province, New Caledonia">North</a> and <a href="/wiki/South_Province,_New_Caledonia" title="South Province, New Caledonia">South</a> Provinces are on the New Caledonian mainland, while the <a href="/wiki/Loyalty_Islands_Province" title="Loyalty Islands Province">Loyalty Islands Province</a> is a series of three islands off the east coast of mainland. New Caledonia's population of 271,407 (October 2019 census)<sup id="cite_ref-censuspop_9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-censuspop-9">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> is of diverse origins and varies by geography; in the <a href="/wiki/North_Province,_New_Caledonia" title="North Province, New Caledonia">North </a> and <a href="/wiki/Loyalty_Islands_Province" title="Loyalty Islands Province">Loyalty Islands Provinces</a>, the indigenous <a href="/wiki/Kanak_people" title="Kanak people">Kanak people</a> predominate, while the wealthy <a href="/wiki/South_Province,_New_Caledonia" title="South Province, New Caledonia">South Province</a> contains significant populations of European (<a href="/wiki/Caldoche" title="Caldoche">Caldoches</a> and <a href="/wiki/Metropolitan_France" title="Metropolitan France">Metropolitan French</a>), <a href="/wiki/Kanak_people" title="Kanak people">Kanak</a>, and Polynesian (mostly <a href="/wiki/Wallis_and_Futuna" title="Wallis and Futuna">Wallisian</a>) origin, as well as smaller groups of <a href="/wiki/Southeast_Asia" title="Southeast Asia">Southeast Asian</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pied-Noir" title="Pied-Noir">Pied-Noir</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Algerians_of_the_Pacific" title="Algerians of the Pacific">North African</a> heritage. The capital of New Caledonia is <a href="/wiki/Noum%C3%A9a" title="Nouméa">Nouméa</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-enc-pres_6-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-enc-pres-6">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=New_Caledonia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: History">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>New Caledonia was part of the continent <a href="/wiki/Zealandia" title="Zealandia">Zealandia</a>, which broke off from the supercontinent Gondwana between 79 million and 83 million years ago.<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> The earliest traces of human presence in New Caledonia date back to the period when the <a href="/wiki/Lapita" class="mw-redirect" title="Lapita">Lapita</a> culture was influential in large parts of the Pacific, <abbr title="circa">c.</abbr><span style="white-space:nowrap;">&#8201;1600</span>–500 BCE or 1300–200 BCE.<sup id="cite_ref-pres-hist_11-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pres-hist-11">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> The Lapita were highly skilled <a href="/wiki/Navigators" class="mw-redirect" title="Navigators">navigators</a> and <a href="/wiki/Agriculturists" class="mw-redirect" title="Agriculturists">agriculturists</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-LoganCole2001_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-LoganCole2001-12">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> The first settlements were concentrated around the coast and date back to the period between c. 1100 BCE to 200 CE.<sup id="cite_ref-pres-hist_11-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pres-hist-11">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Two_Kanak_(Canaque)_warriors_posing_with_penis_gourds_and_spears,_New_Caledonia.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/71/Two_Kanak_%28Canaque%29_warriors_posing_with_penis_gourds_and_spears%2C_New_Caledonia.jpg/170px-Two_Kanak_%28Canaque%29_warriors_posing_with_penis_gourds_and_spears%2C_New_Caledonia.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="283" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/71/Two_Kanak_%28Canaque%29_warriors_posing_with_penis_gourds_and_spears%2C_New_Caledonia.jpg/255px-Two_Kanak_%28Canaque%29_warriors_posing_with_penis_gourds_and_spears%2C_New_Caledonia.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/71/Two_Kanak_%28Canaque%29_warriors_posing_with_penis_gourds_and_spears%2C_New_Caledonia.jpg/340px-Two_Kanak_%28Canaque%29_warriors_posing_with_penis_gourds_and_spears%2C_New_Caledonia.jpg 2x" data-file-width="371" data-file-height="618" /></a><figcaption>Two Kanak warriors posing with <a href="/wiki/Koteka" title="Koteka">penis gourds</a> and spears, around 1880</figcaption></figure>
<p>British explorer <a href="/wiki/James_Cook" title="James Cook">James Cook</a> was the first European to sight New Caledonia, on 4 September 1774, during his second voyage.<sup id="cite_ref-ped_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ped-13">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> He named it "New <a href="/wiki/Caledonia" title="Caledonia">Caledonia</a>", as the northeast of the island reminded him of <a href="/wiki/Scotland" title="Scotland">Scotland</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-ped_13-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ped-13">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> The west coast of Grande Terre was approached by the <a href="/wiki/Jean-Fran%C3%A7ois_de_Galaup,_comte_de_Lap%C3%A9rouse" title="Jean-François de Galaup, comte de Lapérouse">Comte de Lapérouse</a> in 1788, shortly before his disappearance, and the Loyalty Islands were first visited between 1793 and 1796 when Mare, Lifou, Tiga, and Ouvea were mapped by English whaler <a href="/wiki/William_Raven" title="William Raven">William Raven</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> Raven encountered the island, then named Britania, and today known as Maré (Loyalty Is.), in November 1793.<sup id="cite_ref-maritimeheritage.org_15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-maritimeheritage.org-15">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> From 1796 until 1840, only a few sporadic contacts with the archipelago were recorded. About 50 American whalers left record of being in the region (Grande Terre, Loyalty Is., Walpole and Hunter) between 1793 and 1887.<sup id="cite_ref-maritimeheritage.org_15-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-maritimeheritage.org-15">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> Contacts with visiting ships became more frequent after 1840, because of their interest in <a href="/wiki/Sandalwood" title="Sandalwood">sandalwood</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-pres-hist_11-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pres-hist-11">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As trade in sandalwood declined, it was replaced by a new business enterprise, "<a href="/wiki/Blackbirding" title="Blackbirding">blackbirding</a>", a euphemism for taking Melanesian or Western Pacific Islanders from New Caledonia, the Loyalty Islands, <a href="/wiki/Vanuatu" title="Vanuatu">New Hebrides</a>, New Guinea, and the Solomon Islands into <a href="/wiki/Slavery" title="Slavery">slavery</a>, <a href="/wiki/Indentured_servitude" title="Indentured servitude">indentured</a> or <a href="/wiki/Forced_labour" title="Forced labour">forced labour</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Sugarcane" title="Sugarcane">sugarcane</a> <a href="/wiki/Plantation" title="Plantation">plantations</a> in <a href="/wiki/Fiji" title="Fiji">Fiji</a> and <a href="/wiki/Queensland" title="Queensland">Queensland</a> by various methods of trickery and deception.<sup id="cite_ref-kanaka_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-kanaka-17">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> Blackbirding was practised by both French and <a href="/wiki/Australians" title="Australians">Australian</a> traders, but in New Caledonia's case, the trade in the early decades of the twentieth century involved kidnapping children from the <a href="/wiki/Loyalty_Islands" class="mw-redirect" title="Loyalty Islands">Loyalty Islands</a> to the Grand Terre for forced labour in plantation agriculture. New Caledonia's primary experience with blackbirding revolved around a trade from the New Hebrides (now Vanuatu) to the Grand Terre for labour in plantation agriculture, mines, as well as guards over convicts and in some public works. In the early years of the trade, coercion was used to lure Melanesian islanders onto ships. In later years indenture systems were developed; however, when it came to the French slave trade, which took place between its Melanesian colonies of the New Hebrides and New Caledonia, very few regulations were implemented. This represented a departure from contemporary developments in Australia, since increased regulations were developed to mitigate the abuses of blackbirding and 'recruitment' strategies on the coastlines.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The first missionaries from the <a href="/wiki/London_Missionary_Society" title="London Missionary Society">London Missionary Society</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Marist_Brothers" title="Marist Brothers">Marist Brothers</a> arrived in the 1840s.<sup id="cite_ref-abcau_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-abcau-18">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> In 1849, the crew of the American ship <i>Cutter</i> was killed and eaten by the Pouma clan.<sup id="cite_ref-logan_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-logan-19">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Cannibalism" title="Cannibalism">Cannibalism</a> was widespread throughout New Caledonia.<sup id="cite_ref-Knauft1999_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Knauft1999-20">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="French_colonisation">French colonisation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=New_Caledonia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: French colonisation">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Communards#Life_in_New_Caledonia" title="Communards">Communards §&#160;Life in New Caledonia</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Pacific_Islands_home_front_during_World_War_II#Employment" title="Pacific Islands home front during World War II">Pacific Islands home front during World War II §&#160;Employment</a></div>
<p><span class="anchor" id="French_colonisation"></span>On 24 September 1853, under orders from Emperor <a href="/wiki/Napoleon_III" title="Napoleon III">Napoleon III</a>, Admiral <a href="/wiki/Auguste_Febvrier_Despointes" title="Auguste Febvrier Despointes">Febvrier Despointes</a> took formal possession of New Caledonia. Captain <a href="/wiki/Louis-Marie-Fran%C3%A7ois_Tardy_de_Montravel" title="Louis-Marie-François Tardy de Montravel">Louis-Marie-François Tardy de Montravel</a> founded <a href="/wiki/Port-de-France" class="mw-redirect" title="Port-de-France">Port-de-France</a> (Nouméa) on 25 June 1854.<sup id="cite_ref-ped_13-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ped-13">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> A few dozen free settlers settled on the west coast in the following years.<sup id="cite_ref-ped_13-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ped-13">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> New Caledonia became a <a href="/wiki/Penal_colony" title="Penal colony">penal colony</a> in 1864, and from the 1860s until the end of the transportations in 1897, France sent about 22,000 criminals and political prisoners to New Caledonia. The <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Bulletin de la Société générale des prisons</i></span> for 1888 indicates that 10,428 convicts, including 2,329 freed ones, were on the island as of 1 May 1888, by far the largest number of convicts detained in French overseas penitentiaries.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;nb 2&#93;</a></sup> The convicts included many <a href="/wiki/Communards#Life_in_New_Caledonia" title="Communards">Communards</a>, arrested after the failed <a href="/wiki/Paris_Commune" title="Paris Commune">Paris Commune</a> of 1871, including <a href="/wiki/Victor_Henri_Rochefort,_Marquis_de_Rochefort-Lu%C3%A7ay" title="Victor Henri Rochefort, Marquis de Rochefort-Luçay">Henri de Rochefort</a> and <a href="/wiki/Louise_Michel" title="Louise Michel">Louise Michel</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-gb-1_23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gb-1-23">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> Between 1873 and 1876, 4,200 political prisoners were "relegated" to New Caledonia.<sup id="cite_ref-ped_13-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ped-13">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> Only 40 of them settled in the colony; the rest returned to France after being granted amnesty in 1879 and 1880.<sup id="cite_ref-ped_13-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ped-13">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:King_Jacques_and_his_Queen.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9d/King_Jacques_and_his_Queen.jpg/220px-King_Jacques_and_his_Queen.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="258" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9d/King_Jacques_and_his_Queen.jpg/330px-King_Jacques_and_his_Queen.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9d/King_Jacques_and_his_Queen.jpg/440px-King_Jacques_and_his_Queen.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1227" data-file-height="1440" /></a><figcaption>Chief&#160;King Jacques and his Queen</figcaption></figure>
<p>In 1864, <a href="/wiki/Nickel" title="Nickel">nickel</a> was discovered<sup id="cite_ref-gb-1_23-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gb-1-23">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> on the banks of the <a href="/wiki/Diahot_River" title="Diahot River">Diahot River</a>; with the establishment of the <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr"><a href="/wiki/Soci%C3%A9t%C3%A9_Le_Nickel" class="mw-redirect" title="Société Le Nickel">Société Le Nickel</a></i></span> in 1876, mining began in earnest.<sup id="cite_ref-gb-2_24-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gb-2-24">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> To work the mines the French imported labourers from neighbouring islands and from the New Hebrides, and later from <a href="/wiki/Japan" title="Japan">Japan</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_East_Indies" title="Dutch East Indies">Dutch East Indies</a>, and <a href="/wiki/French_Indochina" title="French Indochina">French Indochina</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-gb-1_23-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gb-1-23">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> The French government also attempted to encourage European immigration, without much success.<sup id="cite_ref-gb-1_23-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gb-1-23">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The indigenous <a href="/wiki/Kanak_people" title="Kanak people">Kanak people</a> were excluded from the French economy and from mining work, and ultimately confined to reservations.<sup id="cite_ref-gb-1_23-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gb-1-23">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> This sparked a violent reaction in 1878, when High Chief <a href="/w/index.php?title=Ata%C3%AF&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Ataï (page does not exist)">Ataï</a> of <a href="/wiki/La_Foa" title="La Foa">La Foa</a> managed to unite many of the central tribes and launched a guerrilla war that killed 200 Frenchmen and 1,000 Kanaks.<sup id="cite_ref-gb-2_24-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gb-2-24">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> A <a href="/w/index.php?title=1917_Kanak_revolt&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="1917 Kanak revolt (page does not exist)">second uprising</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/R%C3%A9volte_kanak_de_1917" class="extiw" title="fr:Révolte kanak de 1917">fr</a>&#93;</span> occurred in 1917, with Protestant missionaries like <a href="/wiki/Maurice_Leenhardt" title="Maurice Leenhardt">Maurice Leenhardt</a> functioning as witnesses to the events of this war. Leenhardt would pen a number of ethnographic works on the Kanak of New Caledonia. Noël of Tiamou led the 1917 rebellion, which resulted in a number of orphaned children, one of whom was taken into the care of Protestant missionary Alphonse Rouel. This child, Wenceslas Thi, would become the father of <a href="/wiki/Jean-Marie_Tjibaou" title="Jean-Marie Tjibaou">Jean-Marie Tjibaou</a><sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> (1936–1989).
</p><p>Europeans brought new diseases such as <a href="/wiki/Smallpox" title="Smallpox">smallpox</a> and <a href="/wiki/Measles" title="Measles">measles</a>, which caused the deaths of many natives.<sup id="cite_ref-logan_19-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-logan-19">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> The Kanak population declined from around 60,000 in 1878 to 27,100 in 1921, and their numbers did not increase again until the 1930s.<sup id="cite_ref-gb-2_24-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gb-2-24">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In June 1940, after the <a href="/wiki/Fall_of_France" class="mw-redirect" title="Fall of France">fall of France</a>, the <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr"><a href="/wiki/General_councils_(France)" class="mw-redirect" title="General councils (France)">Conseil Général</a></i></span> of New Caledonia voted unanimously to support the <a href="/wiki/Free_French" class="mw-redirect" title="Free French">Free French</a> government, and in September the pro-<a href="/wiki/Vichy_France" title="Vichy France">Vichy</a> governor was forced to leave for Indochina.<sup id="cite_ref-gb-2_24-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gb-2-24">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1941, some 300 men from the territory volunteered for service overseas. They were joined, in April, by 300 men from French Polynesia ('the Tahitians'), plus a handful from the French districts of the New Hebrides: together they formed the <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Bataillon du Pacifique</i></span>. The Caledonians formed two of the companies, and the Polynesians the other two. In May 1941, they sailed to Australia and boarded the <a href="/wiki/RMS_Queen_Elizabeth" title="RMS Queen Elizabeth">RMS&#160;<i>Queen Elizabeth</i></a> for the onward voyage to Africa. They joined the other Free French (FF) battalions in Qastina in August, before moving to the Western Desert with the 1st FF Brigade (<span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">1<sup>re</sup> BFL</i></span>). There they were one of the four battalions who took part in the breakout after the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Bir_Hakeim" title="Battle of Bir Hakeim">Battle of Bir Hakeim</a> in 1942. Their losses could not easily be replaced from the Pacific and they were therefore amalgamated with the Frenchmen of another battalion wearing the anchor of <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">la Coloniale</i></span>, the BIM, to form the <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Bataillon de l'infanterie de marine et du Pacifique</i></span>. The combined battalion formed part of the <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Gaulliste 1<sup>re</sup> Division Motorisée d'Infanterie</i></span>/<span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Division de Marche d'Infanterie</i></span>, alongside three divisions from the French North African forces, in the French Expeditionary Corps during the Italian Campaign. They landed in Provence in 1944, when they were posted out and replaced by local French volunteers and résistants.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Meanwhile, in March 1942, with the assistance of Australia,<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> New Caledonia became an important <a href="/wiki/Allies_of_World_War_II" title="Allies of World War II">Allied</a> base,<sup id="cite_ref-gb-2_24-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gb-2-24">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> and the main South Pacific Fleet base of the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Navy" title="United States Navy">United States Navy</a> in the South Pacific moved to Nouméa in 1942–1943.<sup id="cite_ref-Rottman2002_27-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Rottman2002-27">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> The fleet that turned back the <a href="/wiki/Imperial_Japanese_Navy" title="Imperial Japanese Navy">Japanese Navy</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_the_Coral_Sea" title="Battle of the Coral Sea">Battle of the Coral Sea</a> in May 1942 was based at Nouméa.<sup id="cite_ref-gb-2_24-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gb-2-24">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> American troops <a href="/wiki/Pacific_Islands_home_front_during_World_War_II#Employment" title="Pacific Islands home front during World War II">stationed on New Caledonia</a> numbered as many as 50,000, matching the entire local population at the time.<sup id="cite_ref-ped_13-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ped-13">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="French_overseas_territory">French overseas territory</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=New_Caledonia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: French overseas territory">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 1946, New Caledonia became an overseas territory.<sup id="cite_ref-ped_13-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ped-13">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> By 1953, <a href="/wiki/French_citizenship" class="mw-redirect" title="French citizenship">French citizenship</a> had been granted to all New Caledonians, regardless of ethnicity.<sup id="cite_ref-ncbrit_28-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ncbrit-28">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During the late 1940s and early 1950s, New Caledonia strengthened its economic links with Australia, particularly as turmoil within France and its empire weakened New Caledonia's traditional economic links to metropolitan France; New Caledonia supplied nickel to Australia in exchange for coal vital for smelting nickel. New Caledonian exports of iron ore and timber to Australia also increased during this time period.<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The European and <a href="/wiki/Polynesians" title="Polynesians">Polynesian</a> populations gradually increased in the years leading to the nickel boom of 1969–1972, and the indigenous <a href="/wiki/Kanak_people" title="Kanak people">Kanak</a> Melanesians became a minority, though they were still the largest ethnic group.<sup id="cite_ref-ncbrit_28-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ncbrit-28">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Between 1976 and 1988, conflicts between French government actions and the Kanak independence movement saw periods of serious violence and disorder.<sup id="cite_ref-ped_13-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ped-13">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> In 1983, a statute of "enlarged autonomy" for the territory proposed a five-year transition period and a referendum in 1989. In March 1984, the Front Indépendantiste, a Kanak resistance group, seized farms and the <a href="/wiki/Kanak_and_Socialist_National_Liberation_Front" title="Kanak and Socialist National Liberation Front">Kanak and Socialist National Liberation Front</a> (FLNKS) formed a provisional government. In January 1985, the French Socialist government offered sovereignty to the Kanaks and legal protection for European settlers. The plan faltered as violence escalated. The government declared a state of emergency; however, regional elections went ahead, and the FLNKS won control of three out of four provinces. The centre-right government elected in France in March 1986 began eroding the arrangements established under the Socialists, redistributing lands mostly without consideration of native land claims, resulting in over two-thirds going to Europeans and less than a third to the Kanaks. By the end of 1987, roadblocks, gun battles and the destruction of property culminated in the <a href="/wiki/Ouv%C3%A9a_cave_hostage_taking" title="Ouvéa cave hostage taking">Ouvéa cave hostage taking</a>, a dramatic hostage crisis on the eve of the presidential elections in France. Pro-independence militants on <a href="/wiki/Ouv%C3%A9a" title="Ouvéa">Ouvéa</a> killed four gendarmes and took 27 hostage. The military assaulted the cave to rescue the hostages. Nineteen Kanak hostage takers were killed and another three died in custody, while two soldiers were killed during the assault.<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Two_official_flags_of_New_Caledonia_on_same_flagpole.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a3/Two_official_flags_of_New_Caledonia_on_same_flagpole.png/220px-Two_official_flags_of_New_Caledonia_on_same_flagpole.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a3/Two_official_flags_of_New_Caledonia_on_same_flagpole.png/330px-Two_official_flags_of_New_Caledonia_on_same_flagpole.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a3/Two_official_flags_of_New_Caledonia_on_same_flagpole.png/440px-Two_official_flags_of_New_Caledonia_on_same_flagpole.png 2x" data-file-width="2296" data-file-height="1722" /></a><figcaption>Flags side by side on the same pole, Nouméa, March 2011</figcaption></figure>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Matignon_Agreements_(1988)" title="Matignon Agreements (1988)">Matignon Agreements</a>, signed on 26 June 1988, ensured a decade of stability. The <a href="/wiki/Noum%C3%A9a_Accord" title="Nouméa Accord">Nouméa Accord</a>, signed 5 May 1998, set the groundwork for a 20-year transition that gradually transfers competences to the local government.<sup id="cite_ref-ped_13-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ped-13">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2008, six lagoons of the <a href="/wiki/New_Caledonian_barrier_reef" title="New Caledonian barrier reef">New Caledonian barrier reef</a>, the world's longest continuous barrier reef system, were inscribed on the <a href="/wiki/UNESCO_World_Heritage_List" class="mw-redirect" title="UNESCO World Heritage List">UNESCO World Heritage List</a>.
</p><p>Following the timeline set by the Nouméa Accord that stated a vote must take place by the end of 2018, the groundwork was laid for a <a href="/wiki/2018_New_Caledonian_independence_referendum" title="2018 New Caledonian independence referendum">referendum on full independence from France</a> at a meeting chaired by the French Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/%C3%89douard_Philippe" title="Édouard Philippe">Édouard Philippe</a> on 2 November 2017, to be held by November 2018. Voter list eligibility was the subject of a long dispute, but the details were resolved in an electoral list that granted automatic eligibility to voters of Kanak origin but excluded those of other origins who had not been longtime residents of the territory.<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> The referendum was held <a href="/wiki/2018_New_Caledonian_independence_referendum" title="2018 New Caledonian independence referendum">on 4 November 2018</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> with independence being rejected.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Another referendum was <a href="/wiki/2020_New_Caledonian_independence_referendum" title="2020 New Caledonian independence referendum">held in October 2020</a>, with voters once again choosing to remain a part of France.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> In the 2018 referendum, 56.7% of voters chose to remain in France. In the 2020 referendum, this percentage dropped with 53.4% of voters choosing to remain part of France.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/2021_New_Caledonian_independence_referendum" title="2021 New Caledonian independence referendum">third referendum</a> was held on 12 December 2021.<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> The referendum was boycotted by pro-independence forces, who argued for a delayed vote due to the impact caused by the <a href="/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_New_Caledonia" title="COVID-19 pandemic in New Caledonia">COVID-19 pandemic</a>; when the French government declined to do so, they called for a boycott. This led to 96% of voters choosing to stay with France.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Politics">Politics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=New_Caledonia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Politics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_New_Caledonia" title="Politics of New Caledonia">Politics of New Caledonia</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Flag_of_New_Caledonia_Congress.gif" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a7/Flag_of_New_Caledonia_Congress.gif/220px-Flag_of_New_Caledonia_Congress.gif" decoding="async" width="220" height="191" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/a/a7/Flag_of_New_Caledonia_Congress.gif 1.5x" data-file-width="330" data-file-height="286" /></a><figcaption>Logo of the Territorial Congress</figcaption></figure>
<p>New Caledonia is a <a href="/wiki/Sui_generis_collectivity" class="mw-redirect" title="Sui generis collectivity">territory</a> <a href="/wiki/Sui_generis#Politics_and_society" title="Sui generis"><i>sui generis</i></a> to which France has gradually transferred certain powers.<sup id="cite_ref-jc_38-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-jc-38">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> As such its citizens have <a href="/wiki/French_nationality_law" title="French nationality law">French nationality</a> and vote for the <a href="/wiki/President_of_France" title="President of France">president of France</a>. They have the right to vote in elections to the <a href="/wiki/European_Parliament" title="European Parliament">European Parliament</a>. It is governed by a 54-member <a href="/wiki/Congress_of_New_Caledonia" title="Congress of New Caledonia">Territorial Congress</a>, a legislative body composed of members of three provincial assemblies.<sup id="cite_ref-undcol_39-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-undcol-39">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> The French State is represented in the territory by a <a href="/wiki/Colonial_and_Departmental_Heads_of_New_Caledonia" class="mw-redirect" title="Colonial and Departmental Heads of New Caledonia">High Commissioner</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-undcol_39-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-undcol-39">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> At a national level, New Caledonia is represented in the <a href="/wiki/French_Parliament" title="French Parliament">French Parliament</a> by two deputies and two senators.<sup id="cite_ref-anom_40-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-anom-40">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup> At the <a href="/wiki/2012_French_presidential_election" title="2012 French presidential election">2012 French presidential election</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Voter_turnout" title="Voter turnout">voter turnout</a> in New Caledonia was 61.19%.<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>For 25 years, the party system in New Caledonia was dominated by the anti-independence <a href="/wiki/The_Rally%E2%80%93UMP" class="mw-redirect" title="The Rally–UMP">The Rally–UMP</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-undcol_39-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-undcol-39">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> This dominance ended with the emergence of a new party, <a href="/wiki/Avenir_Ensemble" class="mw-redirect" title="Avenir Ensemble">Avenir Ensemble</a>, also opposed to independence, but considered more open to dialogue with the Kanak movement,<sup id="cite_ref-undcol_39-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-undcol-39">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> which is part of the <a href="/wiki/Kanak_and_Socialist_National_Liberation_Front" title="Kanak and Socialist National Liberation Front">Kanak and Socialist National Liberation Front</a>, a coalition of several pro-independence groups.<sup id="cite_ref-undcol_39-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-undcol-39">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Customary_authority">Customary authority</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=New_Caledonia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Customary authority">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Kanak society has several layers of customary authority, from the 4,000–5,000 family-based clans to the eight customary areas (<span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">aires coutumières</i></span>) that make up the territory.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAnaya20118_42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAnaya20118-42">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> Clans are led by clan chiefs and constitute 341 tribes, each headed by a tribal chief. The tribes are further grouped into 57 customary chiefdoms (<span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">chefferies</i></span>), each headed by a head chief, and forming the administrative subdivisions of the customary areas.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAnaya20118_42-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAnaya20118-42">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:US_Navy_090925-N-8721D-037_Capt._Thom_Burke,_commanding_officer_of_the_amphibious_command_ship_USS_Blue_Ridge_(LCC_19)_receives_a_wreath_to_lay_at_the_U.S._war_memorial_during_a_ceremony.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8d/US_Navy_090925-N-8721D-037_Capt._Thom_Burke%2C_commanding_officer_of_the_amphibious_command_ship_USS_Blue_Ridge_%28LCC_19%29_receives_a_wreath_to_lay_at_the_U.S._war_memorial_during_a_ceremony.jpg/220px-thumbnail.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="156" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8d/US_Navy_090925-N-8721D-037_Capt._Thom_Burke%2C_commanding_officer_of_the_amphibious_command_ship_USS_Blue_Ridge_%28LCC_19%29_receives_a_wreath_to_lay_at_the_U.S._war_memorial_during_a_ceremony.jpg/330px-thumbnail.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8d/US_Navy_090925-N-8721D-037_Capt._Thom_Burke%2C_commanding_officer_of_the_amphibious_command_ship_USS_Blue_Ridge_%28LCC_19%29_receives_a_wreath_to_lay_at_the_U.S._war_memorial_during_a_ceremony.jpg/440px-thumbnail.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2100" data-file-height="1486" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Jean_L%C3%A8ques" title="Jean Lèques">Jean Lèques</a> during a ceremony honouring U.S. service members who helped ensure the freedom of New Caledonia during World War II</figcaption></figure>
<p>The Customary Senate is the assembly of the various traditional councils of the Kanaks, and has jurisdiction over the law proposals concerning the Kanak identity.<sup id="cite_ref-cs-fra_43-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cs-fra-43">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> The Customary Senate is composed of 16 members appointed by each traditional council, with two representatives per customary area.<sup id="cite_ref-cs-fra_43-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cs-fra-43">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> In its advisory role, the Customary Senate must be consulted on law proposals "concerning the Kanak identity" as defined in the Nouméa Accord.<sup id="cite_ref-cs-fra_43-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cs-fra-43">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> It also has a deliberative role on law proposals that would affect identity, the civil customary statute, and the land system.<sup id="cite_ref-cs-fra_43-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cs-fra-43">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> A new president is appointed each year in August or September, and the presidency rotates between the eight customary areas.<sup id="cite_ref-cs-fra_43-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cs-fra-43">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Kanak people have recourse to customary authorities regarding civil matters such as marriage, adoption, inheritance, and some land issues.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAnaya20118_42-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAnaya20118-42">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> The French administration typically respects decisions made in the customary system.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAnaya20118_42-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAnaya20118-42">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> However, their jurisdiction is sharply limited in penal matters, as some matters relating to the customary justice system, including the use of <a href="/wiki/Judicial_corporal_punishment" title="Judicial corporal punishment">corporal punishment</a>, are seen as clashing with the human rights obligations of France.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAnaya20118_42-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAnaya20118-42">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Military_and_Gendarmerie">Military and Gendarmerie</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=New_Caledonia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Military and Gendarmerie">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The Armed Forces of New Caledonia (<a href="/wiki/French_language" title="French language">French</a>: <i lang="fr">Forces armées de Nouvelle-Calédonie</i>, or <b><span title="French-language text"><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;">FANC</span></span></b>) include about 2,000 soldiers, mainly deployed in <a href="/wiki/Koumac" title="Koumac">Koumac</a>, <a href="/w/index.php?title=Nanda%C3%AF,_New_Caledonia&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Nandaï, New Caledonia (page does not exist)">Nandaï</a>, <a href="/wiki/La_Tontouta_International_Airport" title="La Tontouta International Airport">Tontouta</a>, <a href="/wiki/Plum,_New_Caledonia" title="Plum, New Caledonia">Plum</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Noum%C3%A9a" title="Nouméa">Nouméa</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-fanc_44-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-fanc-44">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> The land forces consist of a regiment of the <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr"><a href="/wiki/Troupes_de_marine" title="Troupes de marine">Troupes de marine</a></i></span>, the <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Régiment d'infanterie de marine du Pacifique</i></span>. About 80 percent of the 700-member regiment is composed of soldiers on short-term (four month) deployments from metropolitan France. As of 2018, only about 30 personnel in the regiment were locally recruited.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The naval forces incorporate several vessels of the French Navy including: one <a href="/wiki/Flor%C3%A9al-class_frigate" title="Floréal-class frigate"><i>Floréal</i>-class frigate</a>, <a href="/wiki/French_frigate_Vend%C3%A9miaire" title="French frigate Vendémiaire"><i>Vendémiaire</i></a>, the patrol and support vessel <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr"><a href="/wiki/D%27Entrecasteaux-class_patrol_ship" title="D&#39;Entrecasteaux-class patrol ship">D'Entrecasteaux</a></i></span> and <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Auguste Benebig</i></span>, the lead ship of the <a href="/wiki/Patrouilleur_Outre-mer" title="Patrouilleur Outre-mer"><span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Félix Éboué</i></span> class</a> of patrol vessels. The French Navy will further reinforce its offshore patrol capabilities in New Caledonia by deploying a second vessel of the <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Félix Éboué</i></span> class (<span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Jean Tranape</i></span>) to the territory by 2025.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-brestboulogne_47-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-brestboulogne-47">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> One <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Engins de Débarquement Amphibie – Standards</i></span> (EDA-S) landing craft is also to be delivered to naval forces based in New Caledonia by 2025. The landing craft is to better support coastal and riverine operations in the territory.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p>As of the latter 2010s, French naval aviation and air force elements in New Caledonia included two Navy <a href="/wiki/Dassault_Falcon_20" title="Dassault Falcon 20">Falcon 200 Gardian maritime surveillance aircraft</a> (drawn from Flotilla 25F), which are to be replaced by the more modern <a href="/wiki/Dassault_Falcon_2000" title="Dassault Falcon 2000">Falcon 2000 Albatros</a> starting in 2025<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> and two <a href="/wiki/CASA/IPTN_CN-235" title="CASA/IPTN CN-235">Casa CN235</a> transport aircraft and three <a href="/wiki/A%C3%A9rospatiale_SA_330_Puma" title="Aérospatiale SA 330 Puma">Puma</a> helicopters from the Air Force's 52 "Tontouta" Squadron.<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> Prior to 2022, the frigate <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Vendémiaire</i></span> operated the <a href="/wiki/A%C3%A9rospatiale_Alouette_III" title="Aérospatiale Alouette III">Alouette III helicopter</a>. However, with the retirement of the type in 2022, it is being replaced by the <a href="/wiki/Eurocopter_AS365_Dauphin" title="Eurocopter AS365 Dauphin">Eurocopter Dauphin N3</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> In 2022, the <a href="/wiki/French_Air_Force" class="mw-redirect" title="French Air Force">French Air Force</a> demonstrated a capacity to reinforce the territory by deploying three <a href="/wiki/Dassault_Rafale" title="Dassault Rafale">Rafale</a> fighters, supported by <a href="/wiki/Airbus_A400M_Atlas" title="Airbus A400M Atlas">A400M</a> transport aircraft and <a href="/wiki/Airbus_A330_MRTT" title="Airbus A330 MRTT">A330 MRTT Phénix</a> tankers, from France to New Caledonia for a three-week exercise.<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In addition, some 855 personnel from the <a href="/wiki/National_Gendarmerie" title="National Gendarmerie">National Gendarmerie</a> are stationed on the archipelago divided into 4 companies, 27 brigades and several specialized and mobile Gendarmerie units. During periods such the 2021 referendum on independence, these forces have been significantly reinforced with personnel deployed from metropolitan France.<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> The air component includes two <span title="French-language text"><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;"><a href="/wiki/Eurocopter_AS350_%C3%89cureuil" title="Eurocopter AS350 Écureuil">Écureuil</a></span></span> helicopters<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> while the <span title="French-language text"><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;"><a href="/wiki/Maritime_Gendarmerie" title="Maritime Gendarmerie">Maritime Gendarmerie</a></span></span> deploys the patrol boat <a href="/wiki/Vedette_c%C3%B4ti%C3%A8re_de_surveillance_maritime" title="Vedette côtière de surveillance maritime"><i>Dumbea</i></a> in the territory.<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-fanc_44-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-fanc-44">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Status">Status</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=New_Caledonia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Status">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:EU_OCT_and_OMR_map_en.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/72/EU_OCT_and_OMR_map_en.png/220px-EU_OCT_and_OMR_map_en.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="135" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/72/EU_OCT_and_OMR_map_en.png/330px-EU_OCT_and_OMR_map_en.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/72/EU_OCT_and_OMR_map_en.png/440px-EU_OCT_and_OMR_map_en.png 2x" data-file-width="2206" data-file-height="1358" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/Special_member_state_territories_and_the_European_Union" class="mw-redirect" title="Special member state territories and the European Union">special territories of the European Union</a> prior to <a href="/wiki/Brexit" title="Brexit">Brexit</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>New Caledonia has been a member of the <a href="/wiki/Pacific_Community" title="Pacific Community">Pacific Community</a> since 1983 with Nouméa the home of the organization's regional headquarters. Since 1986, the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Committee_on_Decolonization" class="mw-redirect" title="United Nations Committee on Decolonization">United Nations Committee on Decolonization</a> has included New Caledonia on the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_list_of_non-self-governing_territories" title="United Nations list of non-self-governing territories">United Nations list of non-self-governing territories</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> An <a href="/wiki/1987_New_Caledonian_independence_referendum" title="1987 New Caledonian independence referendum">independence referendum</a> was held the following year, but independence was rejected by a large majority.
</p><p>Under the <a href="/wiki/Noum%C3%A9a_Accord" title="Nouméa Accord">Nouméa Accord</a>, signed in 1998 following a period of secessionist unrest in the 1980s and approved in <a href="/wiki/1998_New_Caledonian_Noum%C3%A9a_Accord_referendum" title="1998 New Caledonian Nouméa Accord referendum">a referendum</a>, New Caledonia was granted special status. Twenty years after inception, the Nouméa Accord required an <a href="/wiki/2018_New_Caledonian_independence_referendum" title="2018 New Caledonian independence referendum">referendum on independence</a> which was held on 4 November 2018.<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> The result was that 56.9% of voters chose to remain with France.<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> The Nouméa Accord required <a href="/wiki/2020_New_Caledonian_independence_referendum" title="2020 New Caledonian independence referendum">another independence referendum</a>, which was held on 4 October 2020. The result was that 53.26% of voters chose to remain with France.<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> The third and last referendum permitted by the Nouméa Accord <a href="/wiki/2021_New_Caledonian_independence_referendum" title="2021 New Caledonian independence referendum">was held on 12 December 2021</a>, confirming New Caledonia as part of the French Republic with 96% voting "no" to independence after the vote was boycotted by the bulk of the Kanak population.
</p><p>The official name of the territory, <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Nouvelle-Calédonie</i></span>, could be changed in the near future due to the accord, which states that "a name, a flag, an anthem, a motto, and the design of banknotes will have to be sought by all parties together, to express the Kanak identity and the future shared by all parties."<sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> To date, however, there has been no consensus on a new name for the territory, although <i>Kanak Republic</i> is popular among 40% of the population.<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> New Caledonia has increasingly adopted its own symbols, choosing an anthem, a motto, and a new design for its banknotes.<sup id="cite_ref-monde1_66-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-monde1-66">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> In July 2010, the <a href="/wiki/Congress_of_New_Caledonia" title="Congress of New Caledonia">Congress of New Caledonia</a> voted in favour of a wish to fly the <a href="/wiki/Kanak_people" title="Kanak people">Kanak</a> flag of the independence movement <a href="/wiki/Kanak_and_Socialist_National_Liberation_Front" title="Kanak and Socialist National Liberation Front">FLNKS</a> alongside the <a href="/wiki/Flag_of_France" title="Flag of France">French tricolour</a>, as <a href="/wiki/Flag_of_New_Caledonia" class="mw-redirect" title="Flag of New Caledonia">dual flags of the territory</a>. The wish, legally non-binding, proved controversial.<sup id="cite_ref-congress_67-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-congress-67">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-voeux_68-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-voeux-68">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> A majority of New Caledonian communes, but not all, now fly both flags, the rest flying only the French Tricolour.<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> The non-official adoption made New Caledonia one of the few countries or territories in the world with two flags. The decision to wish for the use of two flags has been a constant battleground between the two sides and led the coalition government to collapse in February 2011.<sup id="cite_ref-fr24_70-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-fr24-70">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Administrative_divisions">Administrative divisions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=New_Caledonia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Administrative divisions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_New_Caledonia" title="Administrative divisions of New Caledonia">Administrative divisions of New Caledonia</a></div>

<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Administrative_divisions_of_New_Caledonia.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/32/Administrative_divisions_of_New_Caledonia.svg/260px-Administrative_divisions_of_New_Caledonia.svg.png" decoding="async" width="260" height="156" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/32/Administrative_divisions_of_New_Caledonia.svg/390px-Administrative_divisions_of_New_Caledonia.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/32/Administrative_divisions_of_New_Caledonia.svg/520px-Administrative_divisions_of_New_Caledonia.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="450" data-file-height="270" /></a><figcaption>Pyramid graph illustrating the administration of New Caledonia</figcaption></figure>
<p>The institutional organization is the result of the organic law and ordinary law passed by the Parliament on 16 February 1999.<sup id="cite_ref-jc_38-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-jc-38">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The archipelago is divided into three provinces:
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/South_Province,_New_Caledonia" title="South Province, New Caledonia">South Province</a> (<i>province Sud</i>). Provincial capital: <a href="/wiki/Noum%C3%A9a" title="Nouméa">Nouméa</a>. Area: 9,407&#160;km<sup>2</sup>. Population: 203,142 inhabitants (2019).</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/North_Province,_New_Caledonia" title="North Province, New Caledonia">North Province</a> (<i>province Nord</i>). Provincial capital: <a href="/wiki/Kon%C3%A9,_New_Caledonia" title="Koné, New Caledonia">Koné</a>. Area: 7,348&#160;km<sup>2</sup>. Population: 49,912 inhabitants (2019).</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Loyalty_Islands_Province" title="Loyalty Islands Province">Loyalty Islands Province</a> (<i>province des îles Loyauté</i>). Provincial capital: <a href="/wiki/Lifou" title="Lifou">Lifou</a>. Area: 1,981&#160;km<sup>2</sup>. Population: 18,353 inhabitants (2019).</li></ul>
<p>New Caledonia is further divided into 33 communes (municipalities).<sup id="cite_ref-jc_38-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-jc-38">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> One commune, <a href="/wiki/Poya,_New_Caledonia" title="Poya, New Caledonia">Poya</a>, is divided between two provinces. The northern half of Poya, with the main settlement and most of the population, is part of the North Province, while the southern half of the commune, with only 210 inhabitants in 2019, is part of the South Province. The communes, with 2019 populations in brackets, and administrative centres, are as follows:
</p>
