<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=New_Caledonia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Geography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_New_Caledonia" title="Geography of New Caledonia">Geography of New Caledonia</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:NewCaledonia2022OSM.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c0/NewCaledonia2022OSM.png/220px-NewCaledonia2022OSM.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="179" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c0/NewCaledonia2022OSM.png/330px-NewCaledonia2022OSM.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c0/NewCaledonia2022OSM.png/440px-NewCaledonia2022OSM.png 2x" data-file-width="6816" data-file-height="5555" /></a><figcaption>Detailed map of New Caledonia</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:New_Caledonia_-_S199828000484.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/80/New_Caledonia_-_S199828000484.jpg/220px-New_Caledonia_-_S199828000484.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="261" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/80/New_Caledonia_-_S199828000484.jpg/330px-New_Caledonia_-_S199828000484.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/80/New_Caledonia_-_S199828000484.jpg/440px-New_Caledonia_-_S199828000484.jpg 2x" data-file-width="734" data-file-height="870" /></a><figcaption>New Caledonia from space</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Reefs_of_New_Caledonia_from_ISS,_September_9,_2020.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4a/Reefs_of_New_Caledonia_from_ISS%2C_September_9%2C_2020.jpg/220px-Reefs_of_New_Caledonia_from_ISS%2C_September_9%2C_2020.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4a/Reefs_of_New_Caledonia_from_ISS%2C_September_9%2C_2020.jpg/330px-Reefs_of_New_Caledonia_from_ISS%2C_September_9%2C_2020.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4a/Reefs_of_New_Caledonia_from_ISS%2C_September_9%2C_2020.jpg/440px-Reefs_of_New_Caledonia_from_ISS%2C_September_9%2C_2020.jpg 2x" data-file-width="720" data-file-height="480" /></a><figcaption>Coral reefs of New Caledonia from ISS, September 9, 2020</figcaption></figure>
<p>New Caledonia is part of <a href="/wiki/Zealandia" title="Zealandia">Zealandia</a>, a fragment of the ancient <a href="/wiki/Gondwana" title="Gondwana">Gondwana</a> super-continent, which is part of <a href="/wiki/Oceania" title="Oceania">Oceania</a>, however it is not part of <a href="/wiki/Europe" title="Europe">Europe</a> but is a territory of the European country of <a href="/wiki/France" title="France">France</a>. It is speculated that New Caledonia separated from Australia roughly 66 million years ago, subsequently drifting in a north-easterly direction, reaching its present position about 50 million years ago.<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The mainland is divided in length by a central mountain range whose highest peaks are <a href="/wiki/Mont_Pani%C3%A9" title="Mont Panié">Mont Panié</a> (1,629&#160;m or 5,344&#160;ft) in the north and <a href="/w/index.php?title=Mont_Humboldt&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Mont Humboldt (page does not exist)">Mont Humboldt</a> (1,618&#160;m or 5,308&#160;ft) in the southeast.<sup id="cite_ref-geo-jc_72-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-geo-jc-72">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> The east coast is covered by a lush vegetation.<sup id="cite_ref-geo-jc_72-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-geo-jc-72">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> The west coast, with its large savannahs and plains suitable for farming, is a drier area. Many ore-rich massifs are found along this coast.<sup id="cite_ref-geo-jc_72-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-geo-jc-72">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Diahot_River" title="Diahot River">Diahot River</a> is the longest river of New Caledonia, flowing for some 100 kilometres (62&#160;mi).<sup id="cite_ref-EB_73-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EB-73">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> It has a catchment area of 620&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (240&#160;sq&#160;mi) and opens north-westward into the <a href="/w/index.php?title=Baie_d%27Harcourt&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Baie d&#39;Harcourt (page does not exist)">Baie d'Harcourt</a>, flowing towards the northern point of the island along the western escarpment of the Mount Panié.<sup id="cite_ref-EB_73-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EB-73">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-UNU_74-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UNU-74">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> Most of the island is covered by wet evergreen forests, while savannahs dominate the lower elevations.<sup id="cite_ref-gbrs_75-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gbrs-75">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> The New Caledonian lagoon, with a total area of 24,000 square kilometres (9,300&#160;sq&#160;mi) is one of the largest lagoons in the world. The lagoon and the surrounding <a href="/wiki/New_Caledonia_Barrier_Reef" class="mw-redirect" title="New Caledonia Barrier Reef">New Caledonia Barrier Reef</a> was named a <a href="/wiki/UNESCO" title="UNESCO">UNESCO</a> <a href="/wiki/World_Heritage_Site" title="World Heritage Site">World Heritage Site</a> in 2008 for its exceptional beauty and marine biodiversity.<sup id="cite_ref-geo-jc_72-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-geo-jc-72">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-unesco_76-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-unesco-76">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> In May 2023 there was an earthquake and tsunami in New Caledonia. This triggered a tsunami warning here as well as in other nearby countries.<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate">Climate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=New_Caledonia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Climate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The climate is <a href="/wiki/Tropical_climate" title="Tropical climate">tropical</a>, with a hot and humid season from November to March with temperatures between 27 and 30&#160;°C (81 and 86&#160;°F),<sup id="cite_ref-geo-jc_72-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-geo-jc-72">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> and a cooler, dry season from June to August with temperatures between 20 and 23&#160;°C (68 and 73&#160;°F),<sup id="cite_ref-geo-jc_72-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-geo-jc-72">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> linked by two short interstices.<sup id="cite_ref-ped_13-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ped-13">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> The tropical climate is strongly moderated by the oceanic influence and the <a href="/wiki/Trade_winds" title="Trade winds">trade winds</a> that attenuate humidity, which can be close to 80%.<sup id="cite_ref-geo-jc_72-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-geo-jc-72">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> The average annual temperature is 23&#160;°C, with historical extremes of 2.3 and 39.1&#160;°C (36.1 and 102.4&#160;°F).<sup id="cite_ref-ped_13-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ped-13">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The rainfall records show that precipitation differs greatly within the island. The 3,000 millimetres (120&#160;in) of rainfall recorded in <a href="/w/index.php?title=Galarino&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Galarino (page does not exist)">Galarino</a> are three times the average of the west coast. There are also dry periods, because of the effects of <a href="/wiki/El_Ni%C3%B1o" title="El Niño">El Niño</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-ped_13-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ped-13">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> Between December and April, <a href="/wiki/Tropical_depressions" class="mw-redirect" title="Tropical depressions">tropical depressions</a> and <a href="/wiki/Cyclones" class="mw-redirect" title="Cyclones">cyclones</a> can cause winds to exceed a speed of 100 kilometres per hour (62&#160;mph), with gusts of 250 kilometres per hour (160&#160;mph) and very abundant rainfall.<sup id="cite_ref-ped_13-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ped-13">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> The last cyclone affecting New Caledonia was Cyclone Niran, in March 2021.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Environment">Environment</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=New_Caledonia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Environment">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Biodiversity_of_New_Caledonia" title="Biodiversity of New Caledonia">Biodiversity of New Caledonia</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Landscape,_south_of_New_Caledonia.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7d/Landscape%2C_south_of_New_Caledonia.jpg/250px-Landscape%2C_south_of_New_Caledonia.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="163" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7d/Landscape%2C_south_of_New_Caledonia.jpg/375px-Landscape%2C_south_of_New_Caledonia.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7d/Landscape%2C_south_of_New_Caledonia.jpg/500px-Landscape%2C_south_of_New_Caledonia.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1600" data-file-height="1044" /></a><figcaption>Landscape in the south of New Caledonia</figcaption></figure>
<p>New Caledonia has many unique taxa, especially birds and plants.<sup id="cite_ref-hjnc_78-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hjnc-78">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> It has the richest diversity in the world per square kilometre.<sup id="cite_ref-hjnc_78-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hjnc-78">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> The biodiversity is caused by Grande Terre's central mountain range, which has created a variety of niches, landforms and micro-climates where <a href="/wiki/Endemism" title="Endemism">endemic</a> species thrive.<sup id="cite_ref-hjnc_78-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hjnc-78">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Largely due to its nickel industry, New Caledonia <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_carbon_dioxide_emissions_per_capita" title="List of countries by carbon dioxide emissions per capita">emits a high level of carbon dioxide per person</a> compared to other countries. In 2019 it emitted 55.25 tons of CO<sub>2</sub> per person, compared to 4.81 for France.<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> The combination of the exceptional biodiversity of New Caledonia and its threatened status has made it one of the most critical <a href="/wiki/Biodiversity_hotspots" class="mw-redirect" title="Biodiversity hotspots">biodiversity hotspots</a> on Earth.<sup id="cite_ref-myers_80-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-myers-80">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Bruno_Van_Peteghem" title="Bruno Van Peteghem">Bruno Van Peteghem</a> was in 2001 awarded the <a href="/wiki/Goldman_Environmental_Prize" title="Goldman Environmental Prize">Goldman Environmental Prize</a> for his efforts on behalf of the Caledonian ecological protection movement in the face of "serious challenges" from <a href="/wiki/Jacques_Lafleur" title="Jacques Lafleur">Jacques Lafleur</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Rally_for_Caledonia_in_the_Republic" class="mw-redirect" title="Rally for Caledonia in the Republic">RPCR</a> party.<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> Progress has been made in a few areas in addressing the protection of New Caledonia's ecological diversity from fire, industrial and residential development, unrestricted agricultural activity and mining (such as the judicial revocation of <a href="/wiki/INCO" class="mw-redirect" title="INCO">INCO</a>'s mining licence in June 2006 owing to claimed abuses).<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Flora">Flora</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=New_Caledonia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Flora">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Amborella_trichopoda_(3065968016)_fragment.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/26/Amborella_trichopoda_%283065968016%29_fragment.jpg/220px-Amborella_trichopoda_%283065968016%29_fragment.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="173" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/26/Amborella_trichopoda_%283065968016%29_fragment.jpg/330px-Amborella_trichopoda_%283065968016%29_fragment.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/26/Amborella_trichopoda_%283065968016%29_fragment.jpg/440px-Amborella_trichopoda_%283065968016%29_fragment.jpg 2x" data-file-width="887" data-file-height="697" /></a><figcaption><i><a href="/wiki/Amborella" title="Amborella">Amborella</a></i>, the world's oldest living lineage of flowering plant</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:0_Araucaria_columnaris_New_Caledonia.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0f/0_Araucaria_columnaris_New_Caledonia.jpg/220px-0_Araucaria_columnaris_New_Caledonia.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0f/0_Araucaria_columnaris_New_Caledonia.jpg/330px-0_Araucaria_columnaris_New_Caledonia.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0f/0_Araucaria_columnaris_New_Caledonia.jpg/440px-0_Araucaria_columnaris_New_Caledonia.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="768" /></a><figcaption><i><a href="/wiki/Araucaria_columnaris" title="Araucaria columnaris">Araucaria columnaris</a></i>, New Caledonia</figcaption></figure>
<p>New Caledonia's fauna and flora derive from ancestral species isolated in the region when it broke away from <a href="/wiki/Gondwana" title="Gondwana">Gondwana</a> many tens of millions of years ago.<sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> Not only endemic species have evolved here, but entire genera, families, and even orders are unique to the islands.
</p><p>More tropical <a href="/wiki/Gymnosperm" title="Gymnosperm">gymnosperm</a> species are endemic to New Caledonia than to any similar region on Earth. Of the 44 indigenous species of gymnosperms, 43 are endemic, including the only known parasitic gymnosperm (<i><a href="/wiki/Parasitaxus_usta" class="mw-redirect" title="Parasitaxus usta">Parasitaxus usta</a></i>).<sup id="cite_ref-futura1_84-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-futura1-84">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> Also, of the 35 known species of <i><a href="/wiki/Araucaria" title="Araucaria">Araucaria</a></i>, 13 are endemic to New Caledonia.<sup id="cite_ref-hjnc_78-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hjnc-78">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> New Caledonia also has the world's most divergent lineage of <a href="/wiki/Flowering_plant" title="Flowering plant">flowering plant</a>, <i><a href="/wiki/Amborella_trichopoda" class="mw-redirect" title="Amborella trichopoda">Amborella trichopoda</a></i>, which is at, or near, the base of the clade of all flowering plants.
</p><p>The world's largest extant species of <a href="/wiki/Fern" title="Fern">fern</a>, <i><a href="/wiki/Cyathea_intermedia" class="mw-redirect" title="Cyathea intermedia">Cyathea intermedia</a></i>, also is endemic to New Caledonia. It is very common on acid ground, and grows about one metre per year on the east coast, usually on fallow ground or in forest clearings. There also are other species of <i><a href="/wiki/Cyathea" title="Cyathea">Cyathea</a></i>, notably <i><a href="/w/index.php?title=Cyathea_novae-caledoniae&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Cyathea novae-caledoniae (page does not exist)">Cyathea novae-caledoniae</a></i>.<sup id="cite_ref-ncd2_85-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ncd2-85">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>New Caledonia also is one of five regions on the planet where species of southern beeches (<i><a href="/wiki/Nothofagus" title="Nothofagus">Nothofagus</a></i>) are indigenous; five species are known to occur here.<sup id="cite_ref-futura1_84-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-futura1-84">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>New Caledonia has its own version of <a href="/wiki/Maquis_shrubland" title="Maquis shrubland">maquis</a> (<i><a href="/wiki/Maquis_minier" class="mw-redirect" title="Maquis minier">maquis minier</a></i>) occurring on metalliferous soils, mostly in the south.<sup id="cite_ref-gbrs_75-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-gbrs-75">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> The soils of ultramafic rocks (mining terrains) have been a refuge for many native flora species which are adapted to the toxic mineral content of the soils, to which most foreign species of plants are poorly suited, which has therefore prevented invasion into the habitat or displacement of indigenous plants.<sup id="cite_ref-futura1_84-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-futura1-84">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Two terrestrial ecoregions lie within New Caledonia's territory: <a href="/wiki/New_Caledonia_rain_forests" title="New Caledonia rain forests">New Caledonia rain forests</a> and <a href="/wiki/New_Caledonia_dry_forests" title="New Caledonia dry forests">New Caledonia dry forests</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-DinersteinOlson2017_86-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DinersteinOlson2017-86">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Fauna">Fauna</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=New_Caledonia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Fauna">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/List_of_birds_of_New_Caledonia" title="List of birds of New Caledonia">List of birds of New Caledonia</a></div>
<p>In addition to its outstanding plant diversity and endemism, New Caledonia also provides habitat for a wide diversity of animals. Over 100 bird species live in New Caledonia, of which 24 are endemic.<sup id="cite_ref-Palmas_87-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Palmas-87">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup> One of these endemic bird species is the <a href="/wiki/New_Caledonian_crow" title="New Caledonian crow">New Caledonian crow</a>, a bird noted for its <a href="/wiki/Tool_use_in_animals" class="mw-redirect" title="Tool use in animals">tool-making</a> abilities, which rival those of primates.<sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> These crows are renowned for their extraordinary intelligence and ability to fashion tools to solve problems, and make the most complex tools of any animal yet studied apart from humans.<sup id="cite_ref-nccbbc_89-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-nccbbc-89">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Cagou.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c2/Cagou.jpg/220px-Cagou.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="157" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c2/Cagou.jpg/330px-Cagou.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c2/Cagou.jpg/440px-Cagou.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1132" data-file-height="807" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/Kagu" title="Kagu">kagu</a>, an <a href="/wiki/Endemic" class="mw-redirect" title="Endemic">endemic</a> flightless bird</figcaption></figure>
<p>The endemic <a href="/wiki/Kagu" title="Kagu">kagu</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup> agile and able to run quickly, is a flightless bird, but it is able to use its wings to climb branches or glide. Its sound is similar to the bark of a dog. It is the surviving member of <a href="/wiki/Monotypic" class="mw-redirect" title="Monotypic">monotypic</a> family <a href="/wiki/Rhynochetidae" class="mw-redirect" title="Rhynochetidae">Rhynochetidae</a>, order <a href="/wiki/Eurypygiformes" title="Eurypygiformes">Eurypygiformes</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-kagu_91-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-kagu-91">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There are 11 endemic fish species and 14 endemic species of <a href="/wiki/Decapoda" title="Decapoda">decapod</a> <a href="/wiki/Crustaceans" class="mw-redirect" title="Crustaceans">crustaceans</a> in the rivers and lakes of New Caledonia. Some, such as <i><a href="/w/index.php?title=Neogalaxias&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Neogalaxias (page does not exist)">Neogalaxias</a></i>, exist only in small areas.<sup id="cite_ref-ncnau_92-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ncnau-92">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Nautilus" title="Nautilus">nautilus</a>—considered a living fossil and related to the <a href="/wiki/Ammonite" class="mw-redirect" title="Ammonite">ammonites</a>, which became extinct at the end of the Mesozoic era—occurs in Pacific waters around New Caledonia.<sup id="cite_ref-ncnau_92-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ncnau-92">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup> There is a large diversity of <a href="/wiki/List_of_fishes_of_the_Coral_Sea" title="List of fishes of the Coral Sea">marine fish</a> in the surrounding waters, which are within the extents of the <a href="/wiki/Coral_Sea" title="Coral Sea">Coral Sea</a>.
</p><p>Despite its large number of bird, reptile, and fish species, New Caledonia has remarkably few mammal species: nine, of which six are endemic.<sup id="cite_ref-Palmas_87-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Palmas-87">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Several species of New Caledonia are remarkable for their size: <i><a href="/wiki/Ducula_goliath" class="mw-redirect" title="Ducula goliath">Ducula goliath</a></i> is the largest extant species of arboreal pigeon; <i><a href="/wiki/Rhacodactylus_leachianus" title="Rhacodactylus leachianus">Rhacodactylus leachianus</a></i>, the largest gecko in the world; <i><a href="/wiki/Phoboscincus_bocourti" class="mw-redirect" title="Phoboscincus bocourti">Phoboscincus bocourti</a></i>, a large skink thought to be extinct until <a href="/wiki/Lazarus_taxon" title="Lazarus taxon">rediscovered</a> in 2003.<sup id="cite_ref-ncnau_92-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ncnau-92">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Much of New Caledonia's fauna present before human settlement is now extinct, including <i><a href="/wiki/Sylviornis" title="Sylviornis">Sylviornis</a></i>, a bird over a metre tall not closely related to any living species, and <i><a href="/wiki/Meiolania" title="Meiolania">Meiolania</a></i>, a giant horned turtle that diverged from living turtles during the Jurassic period.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=New_Caledonia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Demographics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_New_Caledonia" title="Demographics of New Caledonia">Demographics of New Caledonia</a></div>
<table class="toccolours" style="width:15em;border-top-width:0;border-spacing: 0;float:right;clear:right;margin:0.5em 0 1em 0.5em;"><caption style="border-top:1px #aaa solid;border-left:1px #aaa solid;border-right:1px #aaa solid;background-color:lavender;padding:0.25em;font-weight:bold">Historical populations</caption><tbody><tr style="font-size:95%"><th style="border-bottom:1px solid style="text-align:right;padding:1px">68,480</td><td style="text-align:right;padding:1px">—&#160;&#160;&#160;&#160;</td></tr><tr><th style="text-align:center;padding:1px">1963 </th><td style="text-align:right;padding:1px">100,579</td><td style="text-align:right;padding:1px">+2.60%</td></tr><tr><th style="text-align:center;padding:1px">1976 </th><td style="text-align:right;padding:1px">133,233</td><td style="text-align:right;padding:1px">+4.03%</td></tr><tr><th style="text-align:center;padding:1px;border-bottom:1px solid #bbbbbb">1983 </th><td style="text-align:right;padding:1px;border-bottom:1px solid #bbbbbb">145,368</td><td style="text-align:right;padding:1px;border-bottom:1px solid #bbbbbb">+1.26%</td></tr><tr><th style="text-align:center;padding:1px">1989 </th><td style="text-align:right;padding:1px">164,173</td><td style="text-align:right;padding:1px">+2.06%</td></tr><tr><th style="text-align:center;padding:1px">1996 </th><td style="text-align:right;padding:1px">196,836</td><td style="text-align:right;padding:1px">+2.61%</td></tr><tr><th style="text-align:center;padding:1px">2009 </th><td style="text-align:right;padding:1px">245,580</td><td style="text-align:right;padding:1px">+1.68%</td></tr><tr><th style="text-align:center;padding:1px">2014 </th><td style="text-align:right;padding:1px">268,767</td><td style="text-align:right;padding:1px">+1.79%</td></tr><tr><th style="text-align:center;padding:1px">2019 </th><td style="text-align:right;padding:1px">271,407</td><td style="text-align:right;padding:1px">+0.19%</td></tr><tr><td colspan="3" style="border-top:1px solid black;font-size:85%;text-align:left">ISEE<sup id="cite_ref-The_Population_at_Different_Censuse_93-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_Population_at_Different_Censuse-93">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>At the last census in 2019, New Caledonia had a population of 271,407.<sup id="cite_ref-censuspop_9-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-censuspop-9">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> Of these, 18,353 lived in the <a href="/wiki/Loyalty_Islands_Province" title="Loyalty Islands Province">Loyalty Islands Province</a>, 49,910 in the <a href="/wiki/North_Province,_New_Caledonia" title="North Province, New Caledonia">North Province</a>, and 203,144 in the <a href="/wiki/South_Province,_New_Caledonia" title="South Province, New Caledonia">South Province</a>. Population growth has slowed down recently with a yearly increase of 0.2% between 2014 and 2019.<sup id="cite_ref-The_Population_at_Different_Censuse_93-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_Population_at_Different_Censuse-93">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Population growth is higher in the North Province (0.3% per year between 2014 and 2019) than in the Loyalty Islands (0.1%) and South Provinces (−0.2%).<sup id="cite_ref-The_Population_at_Different_Censuse_93-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_Population_at_Different_Censuse-93">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>30% of the population was under the age of 20,<sup id="cite_ref-:0_94-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-94">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup> with the ratio of older people in the total population increasing.<sup id="cite_ref-insee-1_95-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-insee-1-95">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> Two residents of New Caledonia out of three live in <a href="/wiki/Noum%C3%A9a" title="Nouméa">Greater Nouméa</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-insee-1_95-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-insee-1-95">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> 78% were born in New Caledonia.<sup id="cite_ref-insee-1_95-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-insee-1-95">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Total_fertility_rate" title="Total fertility rate">total fertility rate</a> decreased from 2.2 children per woman in 2014 to 1.9 in 2019.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_94-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-94">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Ethnic_groups">Ethnic groups</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=New_Caledonia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Ethnic groups">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="barbox tright" style="overflow-x: auto;">

</div>
<p>At the 2019 census,<sup id="cite_ref-Population_Structure_of_Communities_96-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Population_Structure_of_Communities-96">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> 41.2% of the population reported belonging to the <a href="/wiki/Kanak_people" title="Kanak people">Kanak</a> community (up from 39.1% at the 2014 census<sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup>) and 24.1% to the <a href="/wiki/Europe" title="Europe">European</a> (<a href="/wiki/Caldoche" title="Caldoche">Caldoche</a> and <a href="/wiki/Zoreilles" title="Zoreilles">Zoreille</a>) community (down from 27.2% at the 2014 census). A further 7.5% of the population either self-identified as "Caledonian" or refused to declare an ethnic group (down from 9.9% at the 2014 census). Most of the people who self-identify as "Caledonian" or refuse to declare an ethnic group are thought to be ethnically European.<sup id="cite_ref-LoganCole2001b_98-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-LoganCole2001b-98">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The other self-reported communities were <a href="/wiki/Wallis_and_Futuna" title="Wallis and Futuna">Wallisians and Futunians</a> (8.3% of the total population, up from 8.2% at the 2014 census), <a href="/wiki/Overseas_Indonesian" class="mw-redirect" title="Overseas Indonesian">Indonesians</a> who are from the <a href="/wiki/Javanese_New_Caledonians" title="Javanese New Caledonians">Javanese ethnic group</a> (1.4% of the total population, the same as in 2014),<sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Tahitians" title="Tahitians">Tahitians</a> (2.0% of the total population, down from 2.1% at the 2014 census), <a href="/wiki/Ni-Vanuatu" title="Ni-Vanuatu">Ni-Vanuatu</a> (0.9%, down from 1.0% at the 2014 census), <a href="/wiki/Vietnamese_people" title="Vietnamese people">Vietnamese</a> (0.8%, down from 0.9% at the 2014 census), and other Asians (primarily <a href="/wiki/Overseas_Chinese" title="Overseas Chinese">ethnic Chinese</a>; 0.4% of the total population, the same as in 2014).
</p><p>11.3% of the population reported belonging to multiple communities (<a href="/wiki/Multiracial_people" title="Multiracial people">mixed race</a>) (up from 8.6% at the 2014 census). The question on community belonging, which had been left out of the 2004 census, was reintroduced in 2009 under a new formulation, different from the 1996 census, allowing multiple choices (mixed race) and the possibility to clarify the choice "other" (which led many Europeans to self-identify as "Caledonian" in the category "other", or to select several ethnic communities, such as both European and Kanak, thus appearing as mixed race, which is particularly the case for the Caldoches living in the bush, who often have mixed ancestry).<sup id="cite_ref-eth09_100-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-eth09-100">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Finally, 2.1% of the population reported belonging to other communities to the exclusion of "Caledonian" (up from 1.3% at the 2014 census).
</p><p>The Kanak people, part of the ethnic <a href="/wiki/Melanesians" title="Melanesians">Melanesian</a> group, are indigenous to New Caledonia.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAnaya20115_101-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAnaya20115-101">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> Their social organization is traditionally based on <a href="/wiki/Clan" title="Clan">clans</a>, which identify as either "land" or "sea" clans, depending on their original location and the occupation of their ancestors.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAnaya20115_101-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAnaya20115-101">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> According to the 2019 census, the Kanak constitute 95% of the population in the <a href="/wiki/Loyalty_Islands_Province" title="Loyalty Islands Province">Loyalty Islands Province</a>, 72% in the North Province and 29% in the South Province.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAnaya20115_101-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAnaya20115-101">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> The Kanak tend to be of lower socio-economic status than the Europeans and other settlers.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAnaya20115_101-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAnaya20115-101">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-isee.nc_102-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-isee.nc-102">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Europeans_in_Oceania" title="Europeans in Oceania">Europeans</a> first settled in New Caledonia when France established a penal colony on the archipelago.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAnaya20115_101-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAnaya20115-101">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> Once the prisoners had completed their sentences, they were given land to settle.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAnaya20115_101-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAnaya20115-101">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> According to the 2014 census, of the 73,199 Europeans in New Caledonia, 30,484 were <a href="/wiki/Caldoche" title="Caldoche">native-born</a>, 36,975 were born in <a href="/wiki/Zoreilles" title="Zoreilles">Metropolitan France</a>, 488 were born in <a href="/wiki/French_Polynesia" title="French Polynesia">French Polynesia</a>, 86 were born in <a href="/wiki/Wallis_and_Futuna" title="Wallis and Futuna">Wallis and Futuna</a>, and 5,166 were born abroad.<sup id="cite_ref-ridet_103-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ridet-103">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup> The Europeans are divided into several groups. The <a href="/wiki/Caldoche" title="Caldoche">Caldoches</a> are usually defined as those born in New Caledonia who have ancestral ties that span back to the early French settlers.<sup id="cite_ref-LoganCole2001b_98-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-LoganCole2001b-98">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup> They often settled in the rural areas of the western coast of Grande Terre, where many continue to run large cattle properties.<sup id="cite_ref-LoganCole2001b_98-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-LoganCole2001b-98">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Distinct from the Caldoches are those who were born in New Caledonia from families that had settled more recently, and are called simply Caledonians.<sup id="cite_ref-LoganCole2001_12-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-LoganCole2001-12">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Metropolitan_France" title="Metropolitan France">Metropolitan French</a>-born migrants who come to New Caledonia are called <i>Métros</i> or <i>Zoreilles</i>, indicating their origins in metropolitan France.<sup id="cite_ref-LoganCole2001_12-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-LoganCole2001-12">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> There is also a community of about 2,000<sup id="cite_ref-LoganCole2001_12-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-LoganCole2001-12">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Pied-Noir" title="Pied-Noir">pieds noirs</a>, descended from European settlers in France's former North African colonies;<sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> some of them are prominent in anti-independence politics, including <a href="/wiki/Pierre_Maresca" title="Pierre Maresca">Pierre Maresca</a>, a leader of the <a href="/wiki/Rally_for_Caledonia_in_the_Republic" class="mw-redirect" title="Rally for Caledonia in the Republic">RPCR</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A 2015 documentary by <a href="/wiki/Al_Jazeera_English" title="Al Jazeera English">Al Jazeera English</a> asserted that up to 10%<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Accuracy_dispute#Disputed_statement" title="Wikipedia:Accuracy dispute"><span title="The material near this tag is possibly inaccurate or nonfactual. (September 2015)">dubious</span></a>&#32;<span class="metadata"> &#8211; <a href="/wiki/Talk:New_Caledonia#Dubious" title="Talk:New Caledonia">discuss</a></span></i>&#93;</sup> of New Caledonia's population is descended from around 2,000 <a href="/wiki/Arabs" title="Arabs">Arab</a>-<a href="/wiki/Imazighen" class="mw-redirect" title="Imazighen">Berber</a> people deported from <a href="/wiki/French_Algeria" title="French Algeria">French Algeria</a> in the late 19th century to <a href="/wiki/Penal_colony" title="Penal colony">prisons on the island</a> in reprisal for the <a href="/wiki/Mokrani_Revolt" title="Mokrani Revolt">Mokrani Revolt</a> in 1871. After serving their sentences, they were released and given land to own and cultivate as part of colonisation efforts on the island. As the overwhelming majority of the <a href="/wiki/Algerians_of_the_Pacific" title="Algerians of the Pacific">Algerians imprisoned on New Caledonia</a> were men, the community was continued through intermarriage with women of other ethnic groups, mainly French women from nearby women's prisons. Despite facing both assimilation into the Euro-French population and discrimination for their ethnic background, descendants of the deportees have succeeded in preserving a common identity as Algerians, including maintaining certain cultural practices (such as Arabic names) and in some cases <a href="/wiki/Islam" title="Islam">Islamic</a> religion. Some travel to Algeria as a rite of passage, though obtaining Algerian citizenship is often a difficult process. The largest population of Algerian-Caledonians lives in the commune of <a href="/wiki/Bourail" title="Bourail">Bourail</a> (particularly in the <a href="https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nessadiou" class="extiw" title="fr:Nessadiou">Nessadiou</a> district, where there is an Islamic cultural centre and <a href="https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cimeti%C3%A8re_des_Arabes_de_Nessadiou" class="extiw" title="fr:Cimetière des Arabes de Nessadiou">cemetery</a>), with smaller communities in <a href="/wiki/Noum%C3%A9a" title="Nouméa">Nouméa</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kon%C3%A9,_New_Caledonia" title="Koné, New Caledonia">Koné</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pouembout" title="Pouembout">Pouembout</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Yat%C3%A9" title="Yaté">Yaté</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul class="gallery mw-gallery-traditional">

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Languages">Languages</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=New_Caledonia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Languages">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Languages_of_New_Caledonia" title="Languages of New Caledonia">Languages of New Caledonia</a></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/French_language" title="French language">French language</a> began to spread with the establishment of French settlements, and French is now spoken even in the most secluded villages. For a long time the level of fluency varied significantly across the population as a whole, primarily due to the absence of universal access to public education before 1953, and also due to immigration and ethnic diversity,<sup id="cite_ref-rl-lang_107-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-rl-lang-107">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> but the French language has now become universal among the younger generations as shown by the censuses of population. At the 2009 census, 97.3% of people aged 15 or older reported that they could speak, read and write French, whereas only 1.1% reported that they had no knowledge of French.<sup id="cite_ref-cen-lang_108-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cen-lang-108">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup> No questions regarding the knowledge of French were asked in the 2014 and 2019 censuses, on account of the population's nearly universal understanding of it.
</p><p>The 28 <a href="/wiki/Kanak_languages" class="mw-redirect" title="Kanak languages">Kanak languages</a> spoken in New Caledonia are part of the <a href="/wiki/Oceanic_languages" title="Oceanic languages">Oceanic</a> group of the <a href="/wiki/Austronesian_languages" title="Austronesian languages">Austronesian</a> family.<sup id="cite_ref-macc_109-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-macc-109">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup> Eight of these can be chosen by parents as optional subjects for their children from kindergarten to high school (four languages are taught up to the bachelor's degree) and an academy is responsible for their promotion.<sup id="cite_ref-maskan_110-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-maskan-110">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> The three most widely spoken indigenous languages are <a href="/wiki/Drehu" class="mw-redirect" title="Drehu">Drehu</a> (spoken in <a href="/wiki/Lifou" title="Lifou">Lifou</a>), <a href="/wiki/Nengone_language" title="Nengone language">Nengone</a> (spoken on <a href="/wiki/Mar%C3%A9" class="mw-redirect" title="Maré">Maré</a>) and <a href="/wiki/Paic%C3%AE" class="mw-redirect" title="Paicî">Paicî</a> (northern part of Grande Terre).<sup id="cite_ref-maskan_110-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-maskan-110">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> Others include <a href="/wiki/Iaai_language" title="Iaai language">Iaai</a> (spoken on <a href="/wiki/Ouv%C3%A9a" title="Ouvéa">Ouvéa</a>). At the 2019 census, 44.0% of people whose age was 15 or older reported that they had some form of knowledge of at least one Kanak language (up from 41.3% at the 2009 census), whereas 56.0% reported that they had no knowledge of any of the Kanak languages (down from 58.7% at the 2009 census).<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-cen-lang_108-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cen-lang-108">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Other significant language communities among immigrant populations include speakers of <a href="/wiki/Wallisian" class="mw-redirect" title="Wallisian">Wallisian</a>, <a href="/wiki/Futunan_language" title="Futunan language">Futunian</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tahitian_language" title="Tahitian language">Tahitian</a>, <a href="/wiki/Javanese_language" title="Javanese language">Javanese</a>, <a href="/wiki/Vietnamese_language" title="Vietnamese language">Vietnamese</a>, <a href="/wiki/Chinese_language" title="Chinese language">Chinese</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Bislama" title="Bislama">Bislama</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=New_Caledonia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="PieChartTemplate thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:202px">

<div class="thumbcaption">
<p>Religion in New Caledonia according to the <i>Global Religious Landscape</i> survey by the <a href="/wiki/Pew_Research_Center" title="Pew Research Center">Pew Forum</a>, 2012<sup id="cite_ref-Survey_112-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Survey-112">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Red; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Christianity" title="Christianity">Christianity</a> (85.2%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:#FFFFFF; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;No religion (10.4%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Green; color:white;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Islam" title="Islam">Islam</a> (2.8%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Gold; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Buddhism" title="Buddhism">Buddhism</a> (0.6%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Chartreuse; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;Folk religion (0.2%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Orange; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;Others (0.8%)</div>
</div>
</div></div>
<p>The predominant religion is <a href="/wiki/Christianity" title="Christianity">Christianity</a>; half of the population is <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholic">Roman Catholic</a>, including most of the Europeans, <a href="/wiki/West_Uvean" class="mw-redirect" title="West Uvean">West Uveans</a>, and Vietnamese and half of the Melanesian and Polynesian minorities.<sup id="cite_ref-ncbrit_28-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ncbrit-28">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> Roman Catholicism was introduced by French colonists. The island also has numerous <a href="/wiki/Protestant" class="mw-redirect" title="Protestant">Protestant</a> churches, of which the <a href="/wiki/Free_Evangelical_Church" title="Free Evangelical Church">Free Evangelical Church</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Evangelical_Church_in_New_Caledonia_and_the_Loyalty_Islands" title="Evangelical Church in New Caledonia and the Loyalty Islands">Evangelical Church in New Caledonia and the Loyalty Islands</a> have the largest number of adherents; their memberships are almost entirely Melanesian.<sup id="cite_ref-ncbrit_28-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ncbrit-28">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> Protestantism gained ground in the late 20th century and continues to expand. There are also numerous other Christian groups and more than 6,000 Muslims.<sup id="cite_ref-ncbrit_28-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ncbrit-28">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup> (See <a href="/wiki/Islam_in_New_Caledonia" title="Islam in New Caledonia">Islam in New Caledonia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_Faith_in_New_Caledonia" title="Baháʼí Faith in New Caledonia">Baháʼí Faith in New Caledonia</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (February 2017)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>) Nouméa is the seat of the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Noum%C3%A9a" title="Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Nouméa">Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Nouméa</a>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=New_Caledonia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Education in New Caledonia is based on the <a href="/wiki/Education_in_France" title="Education in France">French curriculum</a> and delivered by both French teachers and French-trained teachers. Under the terms of the 1998 <a href="/wiki/Noum%C3%A9a_Accord" title="Nouméa Accord">Nouméa Accord</a>, primary education is the responsibility of the three provinces. As of 2010, secondary education was in the process of being transferred to the provinces.<sup id="cite_ref-CSC_Australia_2010_114-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CSC_Australia_2010-114">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup> The majority of schools are located in <a href="/wiki/Noum%C3%A9a" title="Nouméa">Nouméa</a> but some are found in the islands and the north of New Caledonia. When students reach high school age, most are sent to Nouméa to continue their secondary education. Education is compulsory from the age of six years.<sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Reliable_sources" title="Wikipedia:Reliable sources"><span title="The material near this tag may rely on an unreliable source. (March 2018)">unreliable source?</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>New Caledonia's main tertiary education institution is the <a href="/wiki/University_of_New_Caledonia" title="University of New Caledonia">University of New Caledonia</a> (<i>Université de la Nouvelle-Calédonie</i>), which was founded in 1993 and comes under the supervision of the <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Higher_Education,_Research_and_Innovation" class="mw-redirect" title="Ministry of Higher Education, Research and Innovation">Ministry of Higher Education, Research and Innovation</a>. It is based in Nouméa and offers a range of vocational, Bachelor, MA, and PhD programmes and courses. The University of New Caledonia consists of three academic departments, one institute of technology, one PhD school, and one teachers' college. As of 2013, the university has approximately 3,000 students, 107 academics, and 95 administrative and library staff.<sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-CSC_Australia_2010_114-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CSC_Australia_2010-114">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup> Many New Caledonian students also pursue scholarships to study in metropolitan France. As part of the Nouméa Accord process, a <i>Cadre Avenir</i> provides scholarships for Kanak professionals to study in France.<sup id="cite_ref-CSC_Australia_2010_114-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CSC_Australia_2010-114">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<tr>
<td style="text-align:left;" colspan="3">Sources:<sup id="cite_ref-GDP_3-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-GDP-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-IMF_117-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IMF-117">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup>
</td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>New Caledonia has one of the largest economies in the South Pacific, with a GDP of US$9.44 billion in 2019.<sup id="cite_ref-GDP_3-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-GDP-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> The nominal GDP per capita was US$34,780 (at market exchange rates) in 2019.<sup id="cite_ref-GDP_3-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-GDP-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> It is lower than the nominal GDP per capita of <a href="/wiki/Hawaii" title="Hawaii">Hawaii</a>, <a href="/wiki/Australia" title="Australia">Australia</a>, <a href="/wiki/New_Zealand" title="New Zealand">New Zealand</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Guam" title="Guam">Guam</a>, but higher than all other independent and non-sovereign countries and territories in <a href="/wiki/Oceania" title="Oceania">Oceania</a>, although there is significant inequality in income distribution,<sup id="cite_ref-nzmin_124-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-nzmin-124">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup> and long-standing structural imbalances between the economically dominant South Province and the less developed North Province and Loyalty Islands.<sup id="cite_ref-undcol_39-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-undcol-39">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> The currency in use in New Caledonia is the <a href="/wiki/CFP_franc" title="CFP franc">CFP franc</a>, as of May 2020, pegged to the euro at a rate of 119.3 CFP to 1.00 euros.<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup> It is issued by the Institut d’Émission d'Outre-Mer.<sup id="cite_ref-viep_126-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-viep-126">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:New_Caledonia_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/New_Caledonia_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/220px-New_Caledonia_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="143" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/New_Caledonia_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/330px-New_Caledonia_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/New_Caledonia_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/440px-New_Caledonia_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a><figcaption>A proportional representation of New Caledonia exports, 2019</figcaption></figure>
<p><a href="/wiki/Real_gross_domestic_product" title="Real gross domestic product">Real GDP</a> grew by an average of +3.3% per year in the first half of the 2010s, boosted by rising worldwide nickel prices and an increase in domestic demand due to rising employment, as well as strong business investments, but by only +0.2% per year in the second half of the 2010s, as the local nickel industry entered a period of crisis and the repeated independence referendums have generated economic uncertainty.<sup id="cite_ref-GDP_3-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-GDP-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> In 2011, exports of goods and services from New Caledonia amounted to 2.11&#160;billion US dollars, 75.6% of which were mineral products and alloys (mainly nickel ore and <a href="/wiki/Ferronickel" class="mw-redirect" title="Ferronickel">ferronickel</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-GDP_3-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-GDP-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> Imports of goods and services amounted to 5.22&#160;billion US dollars.<sup id="cite_ref-GDP_3-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-GDP-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> 22.1% of the imports of goods came from <a href="/wiki/Metropolitan_France" title="Metropolitan France">Metropolitan France</a> and its <a href="/wiki/Overseas_department" class="mw-redirect" title="Overseas department">overseas departments</a>, 16.1% from other countries in the <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a>, 14.6% from Singapore (essentially fuel), 9.6% from Australia, 4.5% from the United States, 4.2% from New Zealand, 2.0% from Japan, and 27.0% from other countries.<sup id="cite_ref-exp-imp_127-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-exp-imp-127">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup> The trade deficit in goods and services stood at 3.11 billion US dollars in 2011.<sup id="cite_ref-GDP_3-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-GDP-3">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Financial support from France is substantial, representing more than 15% of the GDP, and contributes to the health of the economy.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_128-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-128">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> Tourism is underdeveloped, with 100,000 visitors a year, compared to 400,000 in the <a href="/wiki/Cook_Islands" title="Cook Islands">Cook Islands</a> and 200,000 in <a href="/wiki/Vanuatu" title="Vanuatu">Vanuatu</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-monde1_66-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-monde1-66">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> Much of the land is unsuitable for agriculture, and food accounts for about 20% of imports.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_128-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-128">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> According to <a href="/wiki/FAOSTAT" class="mw-redirect" title="FAOSTAT">FAOSTAT</a>, New Caledonia is a significant producer of: <a href="/wiki/Yam_(vegetable)" title="Yam (vegetable)">yams</a> (33rd); <a href="/wiki/Taro" title="Taro">taro</a> (44th); <a href="/wiki/Plantain_(cooking)" class="mw-redirect" title="Plantain (cooking)">plantains</a> (50th); <a href="/wiki/Coconut" title="Coconut">coconuts</a> (52nd).<sup id="cite_ref-FAOSTAT_Production_statistics_2008_129-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FAOSTAT_Production_statistics_2008-129">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Exclusive_economic_zone" title="Exclusive economic zone">exclusive economic zone</a> of New Caledonia covers 1.4&#160;million square kilometres (0.54&#160;million square miles).<sup id="cite_ref-pres-om_7-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pres-om-7">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> The construction sector accounts for roughly 12% of GDP, employing 9.9% of the salaried population in 2010.<sup id="cite_ref-nzmin_124-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-nzmin-124">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup> Manufacturing is largely confined to small-scale activities such as the transformation of foodstuffs, textiles and plastics.<sup id="cite_ref-nzmin_124-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-nzmin-124">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
