<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Nickel_sector">Nickel sector</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=New_Caledonia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Nickel sector">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Nickel_mining_in_New_Caledonia" title="Nickel mining in New Caledonia">Nickel mining in New Caledonia</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Geology_of_New_Caledonia" title="Geology of New Caledonia">Geology of New Caledonia</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Creek_South_New_Caledonia.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c0/Creek_South_New_Caledonia.JPG/220px-Creek_South_New_Caledonia.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="144" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c0/Creek_South_New_Caledonia.JPG/330px-Creek_South_New_Caledonia.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c0/Creek_South_New_Caledonia.JPG/440px-Creek_South_New_Caledonia.JPG 2x" data-file-width="2934" data-file-height="1914" /></a><figcaption>A creek in southern New Caledonia. Red colours reveal the richness of the ground in <a href="/wiki/Iron_oxide" title="Iron oxide">iron oxides</a> and nickel.</figcaption></figure>
<p>New Caledonian soils contain about 25% of the world's <a href="/wiki/Nickel" title="Nickel">nickel</a> resources.<sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Late-2000s_recession" class="mw-redirect" title="Late-2000s recession">late-2000s recession</a> has gravely affected the nickel industry, as the sector faced a significant drop in nickel prices (−31.0% year-on-year in 2009) for the second consecutive year.<sup id="cite_ref-comptes_131-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-comptes-131">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> The fall in prices has led a number of producers to reduce or stop altogether their activity, resulting in a reduction of the global supply of nickel by 6% compared to 2008.<sup id="cite_ref-comptes_131-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-comptes-131">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>This context, combined with bad weather, has forced the operators in the sector to revise downwards their production target.<sup id="cite_ref-comptes_131-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-comptes-131">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> Thus, the activity of mineral extraction has declined by 8% in volume year on year.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Dates_and_numbers#Chronological_items" title="Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Dates and numbers"><span title="The time period mentioned near this tag is ambiguous. (October 2017)">when?</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup id="cite_ref-comptes_131-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-comptes-131">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> The share of the nickel sector as a percentage of GDP fell from 8% in 2008 to 5% in 2009.<sup id="cite_ref-comptes_131-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-comptes-131">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> A trend reversal and a recovery in demand have been recorded early in the second half of 2009, allowing a 2.0% increase in the local metal production.<sup id="cite_ref-comptes_131-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-comptes-131">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> A March 2020 report stated that "New Caledonia is the world's fourth largest nickel producer, which has seen a 26% rally in prices in the past year".<sup id="cite_ref-bbc.com_132-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc.com-132">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup> According to industry sources however, the Goro mine has never met its potential capacity to produce "60,000 tpy of nickel in the form of nickel oxide, due to design flaws and operational commissioning issues" In 2019, it produced slightly over a third of its annual capacity".<sup id="cite_ref-mining.com_133-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mining.com-133">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In March 2021, <a href="/wiki/Tesla,_Inc." title="Tesla, Inc.">Tesla</a> agreed to a partnership with the Goro Mine, a "technical and industrial partnership to help with product and sustainability standards along with taking nickel for its battery production, according to the agreement", according to a <a href="/wiki/BBC_News" title="BBC News">BBC News</a> report. The majority owner, Vale, said that the deal will be of long-term benefit in terms of jobs and the economy. Tesla is a heavy user of nickel for making the lithium-ion batteries and wanted to "secure its long-term supply".<sup id="cite_ref-bbc.com_132-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc.com-132">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Also in March 2021, a part of Vale's nickel business was sold "to a consortium called Prony, which includes Swiss commodity trader Trafigura". Provincial authorities and businesses in New Caledonia would have a 51% stake in the Vale operation.<sup id="cite_ref-mining.com_133-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mining.com-133">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=New_Caledonia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Foire_chevaux.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Foire_chevaux.JPG/220px-Foire_chevaux.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="124" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Foire_chevaux.JPG/330px-Foire_chevaux.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Foire_chevaux.JPG/440px-Foire_chevaux.JPG 2x" data-file-width="1920" data-file-height="1080" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Caldoche" title="Caldoche">Caldoches</a>, <a href="/wiki/European_emigration" title="European emigration">European</a> people born in New Caledonia</figcaption></figure>
<p><a href="/wiki/Wood_carving" title="Wood carving">Wood carving</a>, especially of the houp (<i><a href="/wiki/Montrouziera_cauliflora" title="Montrouziera cauliflora">Montrouziera cauliflora</a></i>), is a contemporary reflection of the beliefs of the traditional tribal society, and includes <a href="/wiki/Totem" title="Totem">totems</a>, masks, <a href="/wiki/Chambranle" title="Chambranle">chambranles</a>, or <a href="/wiki/Fl%C3%A8che_fa%C3%AEti%C3%A8re" title="Flèche faîtière">flèche faîtière</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-desc_134-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-desc-134">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup> a kind of arrow that adorns the roofs of Kanak houses. <a href="/wiki/Basketry" class="mw-redirect" title="Basketry">Basketry</a> is a craft widely practised by tribal women, creating objects of daily use.<sup id="cite_ref-desc_134-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-desc-134">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Jean-Marie_Tjibaou_Cultural_Centre" title="Jean-Marie Tjibaou Cultural Centre">Jean-Marie Tjibaou Cultural Centre</a>, designed by Italian architect <a href="/wiki/Renzo_Piano" title="Renzo Piano">Renzo Piano</a> and opened in 1998, is the icon of the Kanak culture.<sup id="cite_ref-desc_134-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-desc-134">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/w/index.php?title=Kaneka_(music)&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Kaneka (music) (page does not exist)">Kaneka</a> is a form of local music, inspired by <a href="/wiki/Reggae" title="Reggae">reggae</a> and originating in the 1980s.<sup id="cite_ref-desc_134-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-desc-134">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/w/index.php?title=Mw%C3%A2_Ka&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Mwâ Ka (page does not exist)">Mwâ Ka</a> is a 12-metre (39&#160;ft) <a href="/wiki/Totem_pole" title="Totem pole">totem pole</a> commemorating the French annexation of New Caledonia, and was inaugurated in 2005.<sup id="cite_ref-mka_135-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mka-135">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Media">Media</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=New_Caledonia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Media">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p><i><span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr"><a href="/wiki/Les_Nouvelles_Cal%C3%A9doniennes" title="Les Nouvelles Calédoniennes">Les Nouvelles Calédoniennes</a></i></span></i><sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup> is the only daily newspaper in the archipelago.<sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-vivmed_138-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-vivmed-138">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup> A monthly publication, <i><span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Le Chien bleu</i></span></i>, parodies the news from New Caledonia.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There are five radio stations: the public service broadcaster <a href="/wiki/Outre-Mer_1%C3%A8re" class="mw-redirect" title="Outre-Mer 1ère">RFO radio Nouvelle-Calédonie</a>, <a href="/w/index.php?title=Oc%C3%A9ane_FM&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Océane FM (page does not exist)">Océane FM</a> (the collectivity's newest station), the youth-oriented station <a href="/wiki/NRJ" title="NRJ">NRJ</a>, <a href="/w/index.php?title=Radio_Djiido&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Radio Djiido (page does not exist)">Radio Djiido</a> (established by <a href="/wiki/Jean-Marie_Tjibaou" title="Jean-Marie Tjibaou">Jean-Marie Tjibaou</a>), and <a href="/w/index.php?title=Radio_Rythmes_Bleus&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Radio Rythmes Bleus (page does not exist)">Radio Rythmes Bleus</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-vivmed_138-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-vivmed-138">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup> The last two stations are primarily targeted to the various <a href="/wiki/Kanak_people" title="Kanak people">Kanak</a> groups who are indigenous to New Caledonia ("Djiido" is a term from the <a href="/wiki/Fw%C3%A2i_language" title="Fwâi language">Fwâi language</a>, spoken in <a href="/wiki/Hiengh%C3%A8ne" title="Hienghène">Hienghène</a> in the <a href="/wiki/North_Province,_New_Caledonia" title="North Province, New Caledonia">North Province</a>, denoting a metal spike used to secure straw thatching to the roof of a traditional Kanak house).
</p><p>As for television, the public service broadcaster <a href="/wiki/France_T%C3%A9l%C3%A9visions" title="France Télévisions">France Télévisions</a> operates a local channel, <a href="/wiki/R%C3%A9seau_Outre-Mer_1re" class="mw-redirect" title="Réseau Outre-Mer 1re">Réseau Outre-Mer 1re</a>, along with <a href="/wiki/France_2" title="France 2">France 2</a>, <a href="/wiki/France_3" title="France 3">France 3</a>, <a href="/wiki/France_4" title="France 4">France 4</a>, <a href="/wiki/France_5" title="France 5">France 5</a>, <a href="/wiki/France_24" title="France 24">France 24</a> and <a href="/wiki/Arte_TV" class="mw-redirect" title="Arte TV">Arte</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-140" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-140">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/CanalSat_Cal%C3%A9donie" class="mw-redirect" title="CanalSat Calédonie">Canal Plus Calédonie</a> carries 17 digital channels in French, including <a href="/wiki/Canal%2B" title="Canal+">Canal+</a> and <a href="/wiki/TF1" title="TF1">TF1</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-141">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup> Analogue television broadcasts ended in September 2011, completing the <a href="/wiki/Digital_television_transition" title="Digital television transition">digital television transition</a> in New Caledonia.<sup id="cite_ref-tnt_142-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-tnt-142">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup> Bids for two new local television stations, NCTV and NC9, were considered by the French broadcasting authorities.<sup id="cite_ref-rnzitv_143-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-rnzitv-143">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> NCTV was launched in December 2013.<sup id="cite_ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The media are considered to be able to operate freely, but <a href="/wiki/Reporters_Without_Borders" title="Reporters Without Borders">Reporters Without Borders</a> raised concerns in 2006 about "threats and intimidation" of RFO staff by members of a pro-independence group.<sup id="cite_ref-bbc-cp_145-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc-cp-145">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sports">Sports</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=New_Caledonia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Sports">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Christian_Karembeu_-_2014.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/Christian_Karembeu_-_2014.jpg/170px-Christian_Karembeu_-_2014.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="255" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/Christian_Karembeu_-_2014.jpg/255px-Christian_Karembeu_-_2014.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/94/Christian_Karembeu_-_2014.jpg/340px-Christian_Karembeu_-_2014.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1807" data-file-height="2710" /></a><figcaption>New Caledonian footballer <a href="/wiki/Christian_Karembeu" title="Christian Karembeu">Christian Karembeu</a>, <a href="/wiki/1998_FIFA_World_Cup" title="1998 FIFA World Cup">1998 FIFA World Cup</a> winner with <a href="/wiki/France_national_football_team" title="France national football team">France</a><sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup></figcaption></figure>
<p>The largest sporting event to be held in New Caledonia is a round of the <a href="/wiki/FIA_Asia_Pacific_Rally_Championship" class="mw-redirect" title="FIA Asia Pacific Rally Championship">FIA Asia Pacific Rally Championship</a> (APRC).
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/New_Caledonia_national_football_team" title="New Caledonia national football team">New Caledonia football team</a> began playing in 1950, and was admitted into <a href="/wiki/FIFA" title="FIFA">FIFA</a>, the international association of football leagues, in 2004.<sup id="cite_ref-147" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-147">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup> Prior to joining FIFA, New Caledonia held observer status with the <a href="/wiki/Oceania_Football_Confederation" title="Oceania Football Confederation">Oceania Football Confederation</a>, and became an official member of the OFC with its FIFA membership. They have won the <a href="/wiki/South_Pacific_Games" class="mw-redirect" title="South Pacific Games">South Pacific Games</a> five times, most recently in 2007, and have placed third on two occasions in the <a href="/wiki/OFC_Nations_Cup" title="OFC Nations Cup">OFC Nations Cup</a>. <a href="/wiki/Christian_Karembeu" title="Christian Karembeu">Christian Karembeu</a> is a prominent New Caledonian former footballer. The under-17 team qualified for the FIFA under 17 World Cup in 2017.
</p><p>The sport of <a href="/wiki/Basketball" title="Basketball">basketball</a> gets much public attention in New Caledonia by both press and fans. Its <a href="/wiki/New_Caledonia_men%27s_national_basketball_team" title="New Caledonia men&#39;s national basketball team">national team</a> has won plenty of medals in the Oceania region.<sup id="cite_ref-148" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-148">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup> New Caledonia's top basketball club teams are <i>AS 6e Km</i> and <i>AS Dumbea</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-149" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-149">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Horse_racing" title="Horse racing">Horse racing</a> is also very popular in New Caledonia, as are women's <a href="/wiki/Cricket" title="Cricket">cricket</a> matches.<sup id="cite_ref-150" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-150">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Rugby_league" title="Rugby league">rugby league</a> team participated in the <a href="/wiki/Pacific_Cup" title="Pacific Cup">Pacific Cup</a> in 2004.  In 2020, plans were formed to create a Rugby League team in New Caledonia, Pacifique Trieze,<sup id="cite_ref-151" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-151">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup> to eventually join the majority Australian <a href="/wiki/Queensland_Cup" title="Queensland Cup">Queensland Cup</a>.
</p><p>New Caledonia also has a national synchronised swimming team, which tours abroad.
</p><p>The "Tour Cycliste de Nouvelle-Calédonie" is a multi-day cycling stage race that is held usually in October. The race is organised by the Comite Cycliste New Caledonia. The race attracts riders from Australia, New Zealand, France, Réunion, Europe and Tahiti. Australian Brendan Washington has finished last three times in the race between 2005 and 2009, and is known in New Caledonia as "The <a href="/wiki/Lanterne_rouge" title="Lanterne rouge">Lanterne Rouge</a>".
</p><p>The New Caledonia <a href="/wiki/Handball" title="Handball">Handball</a> team won the <a href="/wiki/Oceania_Handball_Nations_Cup" title="Oceania Handball Nations Cup">Oceania Handball Nations Cup</a> in 2008 held in <a href="/wiki/Wellington" title="Wellington">Wellington</a>, <a href="/wiki/New_Zealand" title="New Zealand">New Zealand</a>. They beat <a href="/wiki/Australia_men%27s_national_handball_team" title="Australia men&#39;s national handball team">Australia</a> in the final.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/BNP_Paribas_de_Nouvelle-Cal%C3%A9donie" class="mw-redirect" title="BNP Paribas de Nouvelle-Calédonie">Internationaux de Nouvelle-Calédonie</a> is a tennis tournament that is held in the first week of January. Since 2004, the tournament is part of the <a href="/wiki/ATP_Challenger_Tour" title="ATP Challenger Tour">ATP Challenger Tour</a>, and players usually compete as a preparation for the <a href="/wiki/Australian_Open" title="Australian Open">Australian Open</a>. the first Grand Slam of the year.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/New_Caledonia_women%27s_national_volleyball_team" title="New Caledonia women&#39;s national volleyball team">New Caledonia women's national volleyball team</a> won the gold medal on several occasions.<sup id="cite_ref-152" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-152">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cuisine">Cuisine</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=New_Caledonia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Cuisine">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Due to low levels of domestic horticulture, fresh tropical fruits feature less highly in New Caledonian cuisine than in other Pacific nations, instead relying on rice, fish and root vegetables such as taro.<sup id="cite_ref-153" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-153">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup> One way this is frequently prepared is in a buried-oven-style feast, known as <i><a href="/wiki/Bougna" title="Bougna">bougna</a></i>. Wrapped in banana leaves, the fish, taro, banana and other seafood are buried with hot rocks to cook, then dug up and eaten.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Transport">Transport</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=New_Caledonia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Transport">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p><a href="/wiki/La_Tontouta_International_Airport" title="La Tontouta International Airport">La Tontouta International Airport</a> is 50&#160;km (31&#160;mi) northwest of Nouméa, and connects New Caledonia with the airports of Paris, Tokyo, Sydney, Auckland, Brisbane, Melbourne, Osaka, Papeete, Nadi, Wallis and Port Vila.<sup id="cite_ref-aport_154-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-aport-154">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup> Most internal air services are operated by the International carrier <a href="/wiki/Aircalin" title="Aircalin">Aircalin</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-tppdf_155-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-tppdf-155">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup> Cruise ships dock at the Gare Maritime in Nouméa.<sup id="cite_ref-lptra_156-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-lptra-156">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup> The passenger-and-cargo boat <i>Havannah</i> sails to <a href="/wiki/Port_Vila" title="Port Vila">Port Vila</a>, <a href="/wiki/Malakula" title="Malakula">Malicolo</a> and <a href="/wiki/Luganville" title="Luganville">Santo</a> in <a href="/wiki/Vanuatu" title="Vanuatu">Vanuatu</a> once a month.<sup id="cite_ref-lptra_156-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-lptra-156">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>New Caledonia's road network consists of:
</p>
<ul><li>Route territoriale 1 (RT1), going from the exit from <a href="/wiki/Noum%C3%A9a" title="Nouméa">Nouméa</a> to the Néhoué River, north of <a href="/wiki/Koumac" title="Koumac">Koumac</a>;</li>
<li>Route territoriale 2, on <a href="/wiki/Lifou_Island" title="Lifou Island">Lifou Island</a> and from the <a href="/wiki/Lifou_Airport" class="mw-redirect" title="Lifou Airport">Lifou Airport</a> to the south of <a href="/wiki/W%C3%A9" title="Wé">Wé</a>;</li>
<li>Route territoriale 3, from the junction with RT1 in <a href="/w/index.php?title=Nandi,_New_Caledonia&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Nandi, New Caledonia (page does not exist)">Nandi</a> up to the <a href="/wiki/Tiwaka_River" title="Tiwaka River">Tiwaka River</a>;</li>
<li>Route territoriale 4, from the junction with RT1 near <a href="/w/index.php?title=Mu%C3%A9o&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Muéo (page does not exist)">Muéo</a> to the power plant.<sup id="cite_ref-rtne_157-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-rtne-157">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="See_also">See also</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=New_Caledonia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: See also">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Explanatory_notes">Explanatory notes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=New_Caledonia&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Explanatory notes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1011085734">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist">
<div class="mw-references-wrap"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-5"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-5">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Previously known officially as the "Territory of New Caledonia and Dependencies" (<span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Territoire de la Nouvelle-Calédonie et dépendances</i></span>), then simply as the "Territory of New Caledonia" (<span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Territoire de la Nouvelle-Calédonie</i></span>), the official French name is now only <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Nouvelle-Calédonie</i></span> (Organic Law of 19 March 1999, article 222 IV<sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup>). The French courts often continue to use the appellation <span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr">Territoire de la Nouvelle-Calédonie</i></span>.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-22"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-22">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">As compared to 4,053 convicts, including 1,176 freed ones, in French Guiana at the same date.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1011085734"><div class="reflist reflist-lower-alpha">
</div>

<li id="cite_note-1"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-1">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">"Soyons unis, devenons frères" is officially a national anthem but is generally used only on regal and viceregal occasions.</span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>
